/** * Miss Behavin' — Unified Worker (v127 — harvest: separates 'no sources' from 'never searched') * ============================================================== * Consolidates linesheet-api + category-api + the new Floor Count * API into ONE worker. Shopify's Retail Barcode Labels app then refuses the * variant — "Update barcodes to use only letters, numbers, and basic * symbols" — so no label can be printed for it. Applied ONLY to the barcode fallback. A real GTIN is never * touched, and the SKU is never rewritten — shopify_adjust_inventory matches * restocks on SKU, so changing it would silently break reorders. * * ── v113 (2026-08-16) — blog photo picker: search, category, paging ── * edit_images took one argument (brand) and returned a fixed 24 photos. * An evergreen post has no brand, so it fell through to "newest 20 active * products" and there was no control anywhere to ask for anything else. * On 2026-08-16 that put bracelets and cropped pants in front of a * homecoming-dress post, and the post published with no image at all. * edit_images now accepts category (Shopify product_type), q (free text) * and cursor, returns cursor + has_next, and drops frozen vendors however * they were reached. Bump this on every deploy. const WORKER_BUILD = 'v123 · 2026-08-29 · A DRAFT CARRYING AN UNANSWERED BRACKET IS NOW REJECTED HERE, AND THE WRITER IS TOLD NOT TO PRODUCE ONE. On 2026-08-28 "Do stripes really make you look slimmer?" published to the live blog carrying "[PLACEHOLDER. Three or four real observations from the shop go here...]" under its own heading. The writer is deliberately instructed to flag a section it cannot fill rather than invent one, and that instruction is RIGHT: fabricated first-hand retail detail is the one thing that would make these pages worth less than publishing nothing. What never existed was the step after the flag. TWO faults let it through. (1) editValidate, the blog validator, had NO placeholder check of any kind - the rule everyone assumed was covering this lives in the PRODUCT/metadata rule set and never runs on a post. (2) That rule would not have caught it anyway: its pattern is /\[CONFIRM:|\[FILL\]|\bTODO\b|lorem ipsum/, which does not contain the word PLACEHOLDER. Both are fixed. New check 10 in editValidate fails any draft whose body contains a marker bracket - PLACEHOLDER, TODO, CONFIRM, BLANK, FILL, INSERT, YOUR WORDS, LOREM IPSUM, TK, TBD, XXX - or any unmarked bracket of 12 characters or more, while leaving [1] and [sic] alone; it names each offender in the failure text so the reviewer can see which section is unfinished. The marker set is deliberately IDENTICAL to the one app build 169 blocks Publish on, so the Worker and the app can never disagree about what counts as unfinished. The blog prompt also now forbids the bracket outright and says what to do instead: OMIT the section and its heading rather than mark where a fact would have gone, because an absent section is invisible to a reader while a bracketed one is a defect on a public page. PAIRS WITH APP BUILD 169, which is the half that actually stops a human: the app reads every bracket as a question, asks it in a popup, writes the answer into the body in place, and hard-blocks Publish - and Save on an already-published post - until none are left. Neither half is sufficient alone: this one cannot stop a bracket typed in by hand after generation, and the app one cannot stop the model producing them. deploy_check reads V123-NO-PLACEHOLDERS. Previous: v122 · 2026-08-28 · THE CATEGORY DROPDOWN NOW PUTS THE TYPE IN THE NAME. Reported with a screenshot: a bare-named card read Goldie - PINKSTRIPE while its sibling read Wren Dress - MINT, and Category was set. The app sends the live Category as `type` (app build 162), but MB_NOUN_BY_TYPE only knew the singular extraction enum, so Dresses, Swimwear and Accessories - the labels the dropdown actually offers - resolved to nothing and the names came back bare even when the person had already said what the item is. Every Category label now maps to its noun: Dresses to Dress, Tank Tops and Corset Tops and Blouses to Top, Skorts to Skort, Rompers & Jumpsuits to Romper, Swimwear to Swim, Accessories to Accessory, and so on. Everything from v121 is unchanged: names always come back, bare only when there is genuinely nothing to go on, typed nouns win over everything, the pool and the exact first-word duplicate guard stand. deploy_check reads V122-CATEGORY-NOUNS. Pairs with app build 167, which puts a type picker in the name panel itself. Previous: v121 · 2026-08-28 · THE REFRESH BUTTON ALWAYS RETURNS NAMES NOW. Decided by Brian 2026-08-28, and asked for three times before it landed: the ↻ must behave identically whether or not the invoice printed a product name. generate_name no longer refuses when it cannot work out what the item is. v111\'s principle is kept by a different route: instead of asking first, the Worker asserts NOTHING - when the noun is unknown, the names come back BARE. Sienna, Marlowe, Paloma - the same way the store already names its boots (Monarch, Bodhi, Dixie, Patsy). A bare first name makes no claim about the garment, so no false fact can reach the catalogue, which was the entire point of the old refusal. Typing the item in the app\'s box still works and still upgrades the set to Sienna Bikini Set - it is a refinement now, never a gate. The v120 pool, the exact first-word duplicate guard against live Shopify titles and same-push cards, and pool_exhausted are all unchanged. Invoices that DO print a name are untouched: they resolve a noun exactly as before and never reach the bare-name path. need_noun is no longer returned by anything; app builds 163-166 keep their handling of it as dead code, which is harmless. deploy_check reads V121-ALWAYS-NAMES. Works with app build 165 or later. Previous: v120 · 2026-08-28 · THE FIRST NAMES COME FROM A CURATED LIST NOW, AND THE MODEL IS OUT OF THIS PATH. Asked for 2026-08-28: girls\' first names in the 13-32 register, the sort that reads as current fashion and influencer naming, plus solidly popular American names. The old instruction was: an actual girl\'s name that feels fresh and a little unexpected, popular but not overused. That is vague enough to have produced Windy, Taffy and Octavia, which is what prompted this. NEW MB_NAME_POOL, 180 names, all distinct. Edit it freely - nothing else in the Worker reads that array. ON HARDCODING VERSUS ASKING A MODEL FOR CURRENT NAMES: generate_name has NO WEB ACCESS. It is a plain API call, it cannot look anything up, and its idea of current is a training snapshot nobody can see or edit. A list is also a snapshot, but one that can be read, edited and vetoed, which is the only difference that matters here. The claude-haiku-4-5 call is REMOVED from this path rather than left dormant, because a second naming path is a second thing to keep in step with the pool and with the duplicate guard. Its only job was to pick a first name and append a noun the caller already supplies, which is two lines of code. Naming is now instant and free. THE DUPLICATE GUARD IS STRONGER, NOT WEAKER. It used to be a sentence in a prompt asking the model not to reuse a name. It is now a filter applied BEFORE anything is chosen: mbUsedFirstNames folds every entry in `used` to its FIRST WORD, so Sienna Dress blocks Sienna Top, and the app already sends live Shopify titles, every name offered for this style, and every name taken by another card in the same push. A name that is excluded cannot be returned rather than being politely asked not to appear. A pool exhausted by exclusions returns pool_exhausted with a reason naming the fix, instead of an empty list that reads as a failure. UNCHANGED: v119\'s typed noun, v111\'s refusal when there is no noun at all, and the rule that the noun is never the namer\'s to choose. deploy_check reads V120-NAME-POOL. Needs app build 165. Previous: v119 · 2026-08-28 · A NOUN THE PERSON TYPED IS NOW ACCEPTED VERBATIM, SO AN INVOICE THAT NAMES NOTHING CAN STILL BE NAMED. v111 made the noun an INPUT and refused when it could not work one out. That was right and it stays right: a noun the MODEL invents is a fabricated FACT about the garment reaching the live catalogue, which is how a bikini became Olive Sunglasses. But mbNounFor has only two routes - regexes over the invoice wording, and a lookup of type against a CLOSED 15-key map (dress, top, sweater, bottom, skirt, shorts, jumpsuit, set, swim, outerwear, activewear, jewelry, bag, accessory, shoe). A real item this app has no word for - sarong, cover-up, sunglasses - could therefore never be named at all, no matter what the person holding the invoice typed, and every retry returned the identical refusal because nothing about the request had changed. App build 163 added a what-is-it box for exactly this and had nowhere to send the answer; 165 routed it through desc, which only ever works for words the regexes already know. New helper mbTypedNoun accepts body.noun and it WINS over anything inferred. This does not reopen v111: the model still may not CHOOSE the noun, it is handed one - the only change is that a HUMAN may be the one handing it over, and reading a fact off an invoice is not inventing one. The value is sanitised because it lands verbatim in a Shopify product title: control characters stripped by code point, whitespace collapsed, markup and punctuation that would break a title or a filename removed, 2 to 30 characters, and at least one letter. A word typed in lower case is title-cased so bikini set does not ship as Amelia bikini set, while existing capitals are left alone so Cover-Up and PJ Set survive as typed. A value that fails any of those checks falls through to mbNounFor exactly as before rather than being forced through. Older app builds do not send the field and are completely unaffected. deploy_check reads V119-TYPED-NOUN. Needs app build 165, which already sends noun. Carries v101\'s edit_get_article, v98\'s photoless_channels, v97, v96, v95, v94, v93, v92, v91, v90 and v88.'; const API_VERSION = '2026-04'; const SESSION_DAYS = 60; const GRAPH_VERSION = 'v25.0'; /* ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ v117 — OCCASION AND AESTHETIC. A CLOSED ENUM, PROPOSED SERVER SIDE. Master item 92 was settled on 2026-08-23 (Brian): tags are HYPHENATED. `going-out`, never `going out`. Every collection rule already written — the occasions nav spec, the hoco build, the winter formal runbook — is built on the hyphenated form, and the spaced form only ever existed because an uncontrolled prompt produced it. That prompt is corrected in this same version (see SEO_PRODUCT_SYSTEM), so the spaced form now has no source. OCCASION IS NOT AESTHETIC, AND THEY ARE TWO FIELDS ON PURPOSE. Coastal cowgirl is worn to a beach wedding, a bachelorette, a brunch and a country concert. Filing it as an occasion tells Google something false, and the IA work measured it: zero URL overlap across the coastal cowgirl, festival and country concert SERPs (2026-08-01). Do not merge these lists. WHY THIS EXISTS SERVER SIDE RATHER THAN AS THREE SHOPIFY FLOWS. Flow's Contains is a raw substring match, so "Chocolate" reads as hoco and "Corset" reads as set. Here every term is matched on WORD BOUNDARIES, so both are safe and `hoco` and `tee` become usable terms rather than traps. Flow also cannot express AND, so `denim` alone would sweep the catalogue; here `denim` AND `mini` together is one rule. And Flow reads the title only — decision 2 freezes titles and this store's boots are named Monarch, Bodhi, Dixie and Patsy, so the title says nothing. The haystack below is vendor + category + description + fabric + colour + name together, which is the only way a Matisse boot is ever proposed correctly. ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ */ const MB_OCCASION_ENUM = [ 'going-out', 'wedding-guest', 'hoco', 'vacation', 'beach', 'everyday', 'festival', 'country-concert', 'winter-formal' ]; const MB_AESTHETIC_ENUM = ['coastal-cowgirl', 'western']; // Terms are matched on word boundaries against the whole haystack. // `any`: one hit proposes the chip. `all`: EVERY term in the pair must hit. // Kept deliberately tight — precision beats recall. A proposal the owner has // to untick costs more attention than one that was never made. const MB_OCCASION_RULES = [ { tag: 'going-out', any: ['sequin','rhinestone','corset','bodycon','strapless','backless','satin', 'mesh','bustier','glitter','shimmer','slip dress','going out','night out'], all: [] }, { tag: 'wedding-guest', any: ['wedding guest','cocktail dress'], all: [['satin','midi']] }, { tag: 'hoco', // 'hoco' is SAFE here and is NOT safe in Flow. \bhoco\b cannot match // "Chocolate"; Flow's Contains does. This is the single clearest example // of why the proposal moved server side. any: ['hoco','homecoming','semi formal'], all: [['sequin','mini']] }, { tag: 'vacation', any: ['vacation','resort','cover up','coverup','cabana','sarong','kaftan','caftan'], all: [['maxi','linen']] }, { tag: 'beach', any: ['beach','swim','swimwear','swimsuit','bikini','tankini','one piece','sarong'], all: [] }, { tag: 'everyday', // 'tee' is SAFE here and is NOT safe in Flow: \btee\b cannot match // "Sateen" or "Velveteen". Same point as 'hoco' above. any: ['everyday','basic','tee','t-shirt','tank','ribbed','jersey','lounge'], all: [] }, { tag: 'festival', any: ['fringe','crochet','boho','macrame','tie dye','paisley','kimono', 'festival','embroidered','eyelet'], // the spec's own example of logic Flow cannot express all: [['denim','mini']] }, { tag: 'country-concert', any: ['western','cowboy','cowgirl','cowhide','concho','bandana','tartan', 'plaid','gingham','rodeo'], // vendor + garment together. This is the Matisse boot case: the title is // "Monarch", the vendor is Matisse and the category says Shoes. all: [['matisse','boot'],['denim','mini']] }, { tag: 'winter-formal', any: ['winter formal'], all: [['velvet','dress'],['velvet','mini']] } ]; const MB_AESTHETIC_RULES = [ { tag: 'coastal-cowgirl', any: ['coastal cowgirl'], all: [['prairie','dress'],['tiered','midi']] }, { tag: 'western', any: ['western','cowboy','cowgirl','cowhide','concho','rodeo'], all: [['matisse','boot']] } ]; // Word-boundary test. A multi-word term also matches across a hyphen, so // "tie dye" hits "tie-dye" and "cover up" hits "cover-up", which is how these // words are actually written on an invoice. function mbTermHit(hay, term) { const t = String(term || '').toLowerCase().trim(); if (!t) return false; const esc = t.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&').replace(/\s+/g, '[\\s-]+'); return new RegExp('(^|[^a-z0-9])' + esc + '([^a-z0-9]|$)', 'i').test(hay); } // Everything the proposal is allowed to read. Title alone is not enough and // is often actively uninformative (decision 2 freezes titles). function mbOccasionHaystack(style, vendor) { const s = style || {}; return [ s.printed_name, s.name, s.type, s.category, s.printed_desc, s.notes, s.fabric, s.vendor_fabric, s.fit, vendor, s.vendor, ((s.colors || []).map(function (c) { return c && c.color; }).join(' ')) ].map(function (x) { return String(x == null ? '' : x); }).join(' ').toLowerCase(); } function mbRunRules(hay, rules) { const picked = [], why = {}; rules.forEach(function (r) { let hit = null; for (let i = 0; i < (r.any || []).length; i++) { if (mbTermHit(hay, r.any[i])) { hit = r.any[i]; break; } } if (!hit) { for (let i = 0; i < (r.all || []).length; i++) { const pair = r.all[i]; if (pair.every(function (t) { return mbTermHit(hay, t); })) { hit = pair.join(' + '); break; } } } if (hit) { picked.push(r.tag); why[r.tag] = hit; } }); return { picked: picked, why: why }; } /* THE PROPOSAL. It proposes; a person decides. Nothing here writes a tag. Returns the chips to pre-tick and, for each one, the term that fired — so the card can say WHY a chip is ticked rather than presenting a decision with no reasoning attached. */ function mbProposeOccasion(style, vendor) { const hay = mbOccasionHaystack(style, vendor); const occ = mbRunRules(hay, MB_OCCASION_RULES); const aes = mbRunRules(hay, MB_AESTHETIC_RULES); return { occasion_proposed: occ.picked, aesthetic_proposed: aes.picked, occasion_why: Object.assign({}, occ.why, aes.why) }; } /* VALIDATION — the closed enum, enforced at the door. Item 87 is why this is not a style preference: a free-text field is exactly how three different `Miss Behavin` vendor strings came to exist, one of them carrying an invisible curly apostrophe that silently emptied the house-label collection. Do not build a second field with the same failure mode. A value that is not in the enum is DROPPED and NAMED in the warnings. It is never silently corrected into something else and never guessed at. The one exception is the spaced form of an enum value — "going out" for "going-out" — which is normalized to the hyphenated form and reported, because that is the item 92 defect this version exists to close and the value is already unambiguous. */ function mbValidateEnum(values, enumList) { const ok = [], rejected = [], normalized = []; const seen = {}; (Array.isArray(values) ? values : []).forEach(function (v) { const raw = String(v == null ? '' : v).trim(); if (!raw) return; const low = raw.toLowerCase(); if (enumList.indexOf(low) >= 0) { if (!seen[low]) { seen[low] = 1; ok.push(low); } return; } const hyphenated = low.replace(/\s+/g, '-'); if (enumList.indexOf(hyphenated) >= 0) { if (!seen[hyphenated]) { seen[hyphenated] = 1; ok.push(hyphenated); } normalized.push(raw + ' → ' + hyphenated); return; } rejected.push(raw); }); return { ok: ok, rejected: rejected, normalized: normalized }; } const TIKTOK_API = 'https://open.tiktokapis.com'; const CORS = { 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*', 'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type, Authorization', 'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, DELETE, OPTIONS' }; // Minimum role per action. Rank: staff < employee < admin. const ROLE_RANK = { staff: 1, employee: 2, pricer: 2, admin: 3 }; // v92 — RANK IS NOT ENOUGH. Roles are an ordinal ladder, and every action needed // to name, price and publish a sheet sits at 'admin' — next to access codes, // store settings, the GTIN backfill and the catalogue-wide channel sweeps. // Promoting Kelly to admin to let her price a sheet would hand her all of that. // // So a pricer sits at rank 2 and carries an explicit list of the actions the job // actually needs. Everything absent from this list stays exactly as locked as it // was. Read it as the answer to "what can she break": nothing outside these. const ROLE_ALLOW = { pricer: [ 'set_active', 'archive', // save the sheet, archive it on publish 'generate_name', 'generate_seo', // the name suggestions and the SEO copy 'shopify_get_titles', 'shopify_get_types', 'shopify_get_vendors', 'shopify_check_sku', // the duplicate guard runs before every push // v102 — read-only: it names the product a title already matches. Without // it the name-duplicate warning has nothing to show her and the card just // errors. shopify_adjust_inventory is deliberately NOT here — restocking // moves stock, and that stays the owner's call. 'shopify_find_by_title', 'shopify_create_product', // the push itself 'shopify_add_images', 'shopify_stage_upload', 'store_product_photo', // v108 — an uploaded photo must survive her save too 'fetch_vendor_details', // re-pull a photo when the wrong one came through 'handoff_start', 'handoff_done', 'handoff_status', 'handoff_list', 'handoff_open', // v93 — the queue is the job // v107 — she can now send a sheet BACK. handoff_save writes only into the // handoff row she already has open, never into the shared active slot, so // this adds no reach beyond the sheet she was handed. 'handoff_return', 'handoff_save', 'private_labels_list', // read-only; without it the vendor field renders unswapped // v109 — SHE COULD UPLOAD A PHOTO BUT NOT LOOK AT ONE. store_product_photo // was opened to the pricer in v108 so her uploads would survive a save, but // the action that SERVES a stored photo back was left at admin. Every // thumbnail on her screen is <img src="...action=get_invoice_file...">, so // every one of them came back 403 and rendered as a broken image — for the // photos Brian handed her as well as her own. App build 139 opens the // photo/SEO cards on a handoff, which would have shown her that screen with // nothing on it. // She is NOT given the run of this action: getInvoiceFile restricts a // non-admin to the photos/ prefix, so the SCANNED INVOICE stays admin-only. // That is deliberate — the scan shows wholesale costs, and "staff see style // names and retail only" is a rule this app states on its own screens. 'get_invoice_file' ] }; function roleMay(role, action) { const need = ACL[action]; if (ROLE_RANK[role] >= ROLE_RANK[need]) return true; return (ROLE_ALLOW[role] || []).indexOf(action) >= 0; } const ACL = { login: null, // shared get_active: 'staff', // v92 — the handoff. A provider swap // never loses scheduled posts. async scheduled(event, env, ctx) { ctx.waitUntil(socRunCron(env)); ctx.waitUntil(editWeeklyAutoDraft(env)); ctx.waitUntil(gscRankMonthly(env)); // v61 — monthly GSC rank pull // v71 — SEO meta detector. The cron fires every 5 minutes for social, so // minute 0 throttles this to once per hour. .catch is deliberate: a failure // here must never take down the social cron sharing this invocation. if (new Date().getUTCMinutes() === 0) { ctx.waitUntil(seoMetaSweep(env).catch(function (e) { console.log('seoMetaSweep failed: ' + e.message); })); } } }; // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // HELPERS // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function json(data, status) { return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), { status: status || 200, headers: { ...CORS, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' } }); } function b64url(buf) { const bytes = buf instanceof ArrayBuffer ? new Uint8Array(buf) : buf; let s = ''; for (const b of bytes) s += String.fromCharCode(b); return btoa(s).replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, ''); } function b64urlDecode(str) { str = str.replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/'); while (str.length % 4) str += '='; const bin = atob(str); const bytes = new Uint8Array(bin.length); for (let i = 0; i < bin.length; i++) bytes[i] = bin.charCodeAt(i); return bytes; } async function hmacKey(env) { return crypto.subtle.importKey( 'raw', new TextEncoder().encode(env.SESSION_SECRET), { name: 'HMAC', hash: 'SHA-256' }, false, ['sign', 'verify'] ); } async function signSession(env, payload) { const body = b64url(new TextEncoder().encode(JSON.stringify(payload))); const key = await hmacKey(env); const sig = await crypto.subtle.sign('HMAC', key, new TextEncoder().encode(body)); return body + '.' + b64url(sig); } async function verifySession(request, env) { const auth = request.headers.get('Authorization') || ''; // v56 — allow a session token via ?token= for GET links opened directly in a // browser tab (invoice-file viewing), where the Authorization header can't be // set. Still HMAC-verified exactly the same way; header wins when both exist. let token = auth.replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, '').trim(); if (!token) { try { token = (new URL(request.url).searchParams.get('token') || '').trim(); } catch (e) {} } if (!token || token.indexOf('.') < 0) return null; const [body, sig] = token.split('.'); try { const key = await hmacKey(env); const ok = await crypto.subtle.verify('HMAC', key, b64urlDecode(sig), new TextEncoder().encode(body)); if (!ok) return null; const payload = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(b64urlDecode(body))); if (!payload.exp || Date.now() > payload.exp) return null; if (!ROLE_RANK[payload.role]) return null; return payload; // { name, role, exp } } catch (e) { return null; } } async function handleLogin(request, env) { const { code } = await request.json(); if (!code) return json({ error: 'Enter your access code.' }, 400); const raw = await env.CODES.get(String(code).trim()); if (!raw) return json({ error: 'Incorrect code. Try again.' }, 401); const rec = JSON.parse(raw); // { name, role } if (!ROLE_RANK[rec.role]) return json({ error: 'Code has an invalid role. Ask the admin to fix it.' }, 401); const payload = { name: rec.name, role: rec.role, exp: Date.now() + SESSION_DAYS * 864e5 }; const token = await signSession(env, payload); return json({ token, name: rec.name, role: rec.role }); } // ── Shopify GraphQL ──────────────────────────────────────────── // ── v41: MAIN Shopify token via the client-credentials grant ───── // The same 2026 model the blog module already runs in production // (editContentToken). Tokens are minted on demand from the LineSheet app's // client credentials (Cloudflare secrets SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID + // SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET), automatically carry the app's CURRENT granted // scopes, and live ~24h in KV. A future scope change needs no OAuth dance: // release the app version, approve the grant in admin, tap "Refresh // Shopify token" in Store Utilities (or wait for the cache to roll over). // The token value itself is never returned to the browser or logged. // v42 — paste-proofing: secrets arrive from a human clipboard, so trim // whitespace/newlines, sanity-check the shapes (client IDs are 32 hex // chars), call out an ID/secret swap by name, and turn Shopify's HTML // error pages into one readable sentence. function ccCred(v) { return String(v || '').trim(); } function ccLooksLikeId(v) { return /^[0-9a-f]{32}$/i.test(v); } function ccErrText(status, body) { let msg = String(body || ''); if (msg.charAt(0) === '<') { const m = msg.match(/<title>([^<]*)<\/title>/i); msg = m ? m[1].trim() : 'HTML error page'; } return 'Shopify main token exchange ' + status + ': ' + msg.slice(0, 200); } async function mainToken(env, force) { const KEY = 'main_cc_token'; if (!force) { try { const cached = env.CODES && await env.CODES.get(KEY, 'json'); if (cached && cached.t && Date.now() < cached.exp - 120e3) return cached.t; } catch (_) { /* cache miss is fine */ } } const cid = ccCred(env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID); const csec = ccCred(env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET); if (!ccLooksLikeId(cid)) { if (ccLooksLikeId(csec)) { throw new Error('The two secrets look SWAPPED: SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET holds the 32-character client ID. In Cloudflare, put the client ID in SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID and the revealed secret in SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET.'); } throw new Error('SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID does not look like a client ID (expected 32 characters of letters/numbers, no spaces). Copy it from dev.shopify.com → LineSheet → Settings → Client ID and re-save the Cloudflare secret.'); } if (!csec) { throw new Error('SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET is empty. Reveal the New secret on dev.shopify.com → LineSheet → Settings and re-save the Cloudflare secret.'); } const res = await fetch(`https://${env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN}/admin/oauth/access_token`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' }, body: new URLSearchParams({ grant_type: 'client_credentials', client_id: cid, client_secret: csec }) }); if (!res.ok) { const t = await res.text(); throw new Error(ccErrText(res.status, t)); } const j = await res.json(); // { access_token, scope, expires_in } const ttl = Math.max(60, (j.expires_in || 86399) - 300); try { if (env.CODES) await env.CODES.put(KEY, JSON.stringify({ t: j.access_token, exp: Date.now() + ttl * 1000, scope: j.scope || '' }), { expirationTtl: ttl }); } catch (_) { /* caching is best-effort */ } mainToken._lastScope = j.scope || ''; return j.access_token; } // v43 — main token resolution with a 60s in-isolate memo (gql runs in // loops; KV reads on every call would add up). Precedence: // 1. authorized token stored by /oauth/callback (KV main_oauth_token) — // offline tokens don't expire; re-running the authorize link replaces it // 2. client-credentials mint (kept for the future; this app's grant type // currently rejects it, and the failure falls through silently) // 3. legacy static SHOPIFY_TOKEN secret (pre-v41 behavior) let _tokMemo = { v: null, at: 0 }; async function shopifyToken(env) { if (_tokMemo.v && Date.now() - _tokMemo.at < 60e3) return _tokMemo.v; let tok = null; try { const stored = env.CODES && await env.CODES.get('main_oauth_token', 'json'); if (stored && stored.t) tok = stored.t; } catch (_) { /* KV miss is fine */ } if (!tok && env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID && env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET) { try { tok = await mainToken(env); } catch (_) { /* grant not permitted for this app — fall through */ } } if (!tok) tok = env.SHOPIFY_TOKEN; _tokMemo = { v: tok, at: Date.now() }; return tok; } async function hmacHex(secret, message) { const key = await crypto.subtle.importKey('raw', new TextEncoder().encode(secret), { name: 'HMAC', hash: 'SHA-256' }, false, ['sign']); const sig = await crypto.subtle.sign('HMAC', key, new TextEncoder().encode(message)); return Array.from(new Uint8Array(sig)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join(''); } // v43 — completes the authorization-code grant at the app's registered // redirect URL. Spec (verified shopify.dev 2026-07-20): HMAC = SHA-256 over // the alphabetically sorted non-hmac query params joined with &; exchange = // form-encoded client_id + client_secret + code to /admin/oauth/access_token. async function mainOauthCallback(url, env) { const q = url.searchParams; const page = (title, body) => new Response( '<!DOCTYPE html><html><head><meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1"><title>' + title + '' + '' + '

' + title + '

' + body + '

', { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/html' } }); const cid = ccCred(env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID), csec = ccCred(env.SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET); if (!cid || !csec) return page('Setup incomplete', 'SHOPIFY_CLIENT_ID / SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET are not set in Cloudflare (linesheet-api → Settings → Variables and Secrets). Add them, then open the authorize link again.'); const code = q.get('code'), shop = q.get('shop') || '', hmac = (q.get('hmac') || '').toLowerCase(); if (!code) return page('Missing code', 'This page only works at the end of the authorize flow. Open the authorize link and approve.'); if (shop !== env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN) return page('Wrong shop', 'This authorization came from ' + shop + ' — expected ' + env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN + '. Nothing was stored.'); if (q.get('state') && q.get('state') !== 'mb-token') return page('State mismatch', 'Unexpected state value — nothing was stored. Use the authorize link exactly as given.'); const pairs = []; for (const [k, v] of q.entries()) { if (k !== 'hmac') pairs.push(k + '=' + v); } pairs.sort(); const digest = await hmacHex(csec, pairs.join('&')); if (digest !== hmac) return page('Verification failed', 'The callback signature did not match — nothing was stored. Open the authorize link again; if this repeats, re-save SHOPIFY_CLIENT_SECRET in Cloudflare (it is used to verify the signature).'); const res = await fetch('https://' + env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN + '/admin/oauth/access_token', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded', 'Accept': 'application/json' }, body: new URLSearchParams({ client_id: cid, client_secret: csec, code: code }) }); if (!res.ok) { const t = await res.text(); return page('Exchange failed', ccErrText(res.status, t) + '. Codes expire in seconds — open the authorize link again for a fresh one.'); } const j = await res.json(); if (!j.access_token) return page('Exchange failed', 'Shopify returned no token. Open the authorize link again.'); try { await env.CODES.put('main_oauth_token', JSON.stringify({ t: j.access_token, scope: j.scope || '', at: Date.now() })); } catch (e) { return page('Storage failed', 'The token was minted but could not be stored (' + e.message + '). Tap the authorize link again in a minute.'); } _tokMemo = { v: null, at: 0 }; try { await logChange(env, { user: 'oauth', action: 'oauth_token', product_id: '', item_title: 'Shopify main token', field: 'scopes', old_value: '', new_value: j.scope || '' }); } catch (_) { /* log is best-effort */ } const scopes = String(j.scope || '').split(',').filter(Boolean).join(' · '); return page('✓ New Shopify token stored', 'Granted permissions: ' + scopes + '.

Close this tab, then tap Refresh Shopify token in Store Utilities — it should say channels are ready. The token itself lives server-side and is never shown anywhere.'); } async function gql(env, query, variables) { const token = env._TOKEN_PINNED ? env.SHOPIFY_TOKEN : await shopifyToken(env); const res = await fetch(`https://${env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN}/admin/api/${API_VERSION}/graphql.json`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'X-Shopify-Access-Token': token, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables: variables || {} }) }); if (!res.ok) { const t = await res.text(); throw new Error(`Shopify ${res.status}: ${t.slice(0, 300)}`); } const j = await res.json(); if (j.errors) throw new Error('GraphQL: ' + JSON.stringify(j.errors).slice(0, 300)); return j.data; } function userErrs(node) { const errs = (node && node.userErrors) || []; if (errs.length) throw new Error(errs.map(e => ((e.field || []).join('.') || '') + ': ' + e.message).join('; ')); } // Resolve the store location: saved in settings, else first active location. async function getLocationId(env, store_id) { const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT location_id FROM settings WHERE store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id).first().catch(() => null); if (row && row.location_id) return row.location_id; const data = await gql(env, `{ locations(first: 10) { nodes { id name isActive } } }`); const locs = (data.locations && data.locations.nodes) || []; const active = locs.find(l => l.isActive) || locs[0]; if (!active) throw new Error('No Shopify location found'); return active.id; } // ── CATEGORY TAXONOMY — HARDCODED, v29 ───────────────────────── // SOURCE OF TRUTH: missb_category_master_map_2026-07-11.md (GID table) with // the three FINAL owner decisions applied 2026-07-17: "Bottoms" dropped // (always the specific type; generic trouser -> Pants), "Loungewear & // Intimates" removed entirely, Sweaters stays its own node. 16 labels, one // GID each. Name-search resolution is GONE on purpose — first-match search // is what produced the "Blouses" drift. Unknown label -> Accessories (aa-2). const CATEGORY_GIDS = { 'Dresses': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-4', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Dresses' }, 'Tops': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-13', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Clothing Tops' }, 'Sweaters': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-13-12', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Clothing Tops > Sweaters' }, 'Pants': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-12', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Pants' }, 'Jeans': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-9', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > One-Pieces' }, 'Sets & Co-ords': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-11', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-20', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Swimwear' }, 'Activewear': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-1', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > Activewear' }, 'Jewelry': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-6', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Jewelry' }, 'Bags': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-5-4', fullName: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-8', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Shoes' }, // v78 — Skorts is a REAL taxonomy node under Clothing and the store has a // live "Category is equal to Skorts" collection with 11 products in it. The // old alias sent skorts to Skirts, so anything added through the app could // never reach that collection. Exact match only: fuzzy taxonomy search is what caused the // "Blouses" drift, and it stays deleted. 'Skorts': { id: null, lookup: true }, // v79 — added because the store now has a collection matching each of these // by Category. Without them the app could not put a product into Blouses, // Camis + Tanks, Corset Tops or Bodysuits at all. GIDs are resolved once by // exact leaf-name lookup rather than guessed. 'Blouses': { id: null, lookup: true }, 'Tank Tops': { id: null, lookup: true }, 'Corset Tops': { id: null, lookup: true }, 'Bodysuits': { id: null, lookup: true }, 'One-Pieces': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-1-9', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Clothing > One-Pieces' }, 'Handbags': { id: 'gid://shopify/TaxonomyCategory/aa-5-4', fullName: 'Apparel & Accessories > Handbags, Wallets & Cases > Handbags' } }; // v79 — resolve a taxonomy label that has no hardcoded GID. // // v78 required the caller to know the FULL path ("Apparel & Accessories > // Clothing > Clothing Tops > Blouses"). The store's collections match on leaf // names (Blouses, Tank Tops, Corset Tops, Bodysuits, Skorts), and guessing // those paths is exactly the kind of drift the hardcoded map exists to prevent. // So: search by LEAF name and accept only an EXACT, case-insensitive match on // the leaf. If the configured fullName is known, it must match too. If more // than one node has that leaf name, refuse rather than pick — an ambiguous // guess is what produced the old "Blouses" drift. let _taxCache = {}; async function resolveTaxonomyGid(env, leafOrFullName, fullName) { const leaf = String(leafOrFullName || '').split('>').pop().trim(); if (!leaf) return null; const key = (fullName || leaf).toLowerCase(); if (_taxCache[key] !== undefined) return _taxCache[key]; try { const d = await gql(env, `query($q: String!) { taxonomy { categories(first: 50, search: $q) { nodes { id fullName name } } } }`, { q: leaf }); const nodes = (((d.taxonomy || {}).categories || {}).nodes) || []; let hits = nodes.filter(n => String(n.name || String(n.fullName || '').split('>').pop()).trim() .toLowerCase() === leaf.toLowerCase()); if (fullName) hits = hits.filter(n => String(n.fullName || '').trim() === fullName); if (hits.length === 1 && hits[0].id) { _taxCache[key] = hits[0].id; return hits[0].id; } _taxCache[key] = null; // ambiguous or absent — remember so we stop asking } catch (e) { /* fall through to the caller's fallback */ } return null; } // Old/tool labels still in flight resolve to a canonical label, never a search. const CATEGORY_ALIASES = { 'bottoms': 'Pants', 'rompers': 'Rompers & Jumpsuits', 'jumpsuits': 'Rompers & Jumpsuits', 'one-pieces': 'Rompers & Jumpsuits', 'clothing tops': 'Tops', // v78: 'skorts' alias removed — Skorts is its own node 'shirts & tops': 'Tops', 'clothing sets': 'Sets & Co-ords', 'outfit sets': 'Sets & Co-ords', 'handbags': 'Bags', 'hats': 'Accessories', 'hair accessories': 'Accessories', 'intimates & loungewear': 'Accessories', 'loungewear & intimates': 'Accessories', // v77 — the app now sends the STORE'S PRODUCT TYPE as the category (one field // instead of two). These map the store's Type vocabulary onto the taxonomy so // nothing silently falls through to Accessories. 'swim': 'Swimwear', 'set': 'Sets & Co-ords', 'sets': 'Sets & Co-ords', 'top': 'Tops', 'dress': 'Dresses', 'shoe': 'Shoes', 'bag': 'Bags', 'jewellery': 'Jewelry', 'accessory': 'Accessories', 'sweater': 'Sweaters', 'activewear': 'Activewear', 'outerwear': 'Outerwear' }; function categoryEntry(name) { const raw = String(name || '').trim(); if (!raw) return CATEGORY_GIDS['Accessories']; if (CATEGORY_GIDS[raw]) return CATEGORY_GIDS[raw]; const canon = Object.keys(CATEGORY_GIDS).find(k => k.toLowerCase() === raw.toLowerCase()); if (canon) return CATEGORY_GIDS[canon]; const alias = CATEGORY_ALIASES[raw.toLowerCase()]; if (alias) return CATEGORY_GIDS[alias]; return CATEGORY_GIDS['Accessories']; // default per the master map — never a guess } // Resolve a plain category label to its exact taxonomy GID. // v78 — one label (Skorts) has no hardcoded GID because it was never in the // master map. For that case only, look it up by EXACT full name and cache it; // if the lookup fails, fall back to Skirts so the push still succeeds and the // product still lands in Bottoms. Fuzzy taxonomy search stays deleted. async function resolveCategoryId(env, name) { const entry = categoryEntry(name); if (entry && entry.id) return entry.id; if (entry && entry.lookup) { // v79 — resolve by leaf name. The label IS the leaf ("Blouses", "Tank Tops"). const id = await resolveTaxonomyGid(env, name, entry.fullName); if (id) return id; // Fall back to the nearest parent so the push still succeeds and the // product is not silently filed as an accessory. const parent = { 'Skorts':'Skirts', 'Blouses':'Tops', 'Tank Tops':'Tops', 'Corset Tops':'Tops', 'Bodysuits':'Tops' }[String(name).trim()] || 'Accessories'; return (CATEGORY_GIDS[parent] || CATEGORY_GIDS['Accessories']).id; } return CATEGORY_GIDS['Accessories'].id; } // ── Activity log ─────────────────────────────────────────────── async function logChange(env, entry) { await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO activity_log (ts, user_name, action, product_id, variant_id, inventory_item_id, location_id, item_title, field, old_value, new_value, undone) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, 0)` ).bind( new Date().toISOString(), entry.user || '', entry.action || '', entry.product_id || '', entry.variant_id || '', entry.inventory_item_id || '', entry.location_id || '', entry.item_title || '', entry.field || '', String(entry.old_value == null ? '' : entry.old_value), String(entry.new_value == null ? '' : entry.new_value) ).run(); } // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // D1 PERSISTENCE — ported unchanged from linesheet-worker.js // (plus: non-admins get wholesale cost + markup stripped) // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function stripCosts(invoice) { const inv = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(invoice)); (inv.styles || []).forEach(s => { delete s.cost; delete s._markup; delete s._markupMode; }); return inv; } async function getActive(env, store_id, session) { let row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM active_invoice WHERE store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id).first(); // v107 — a store with no active sheet used to return data:null and with it the // whole handoff queue, so the screen was told nothing was waiting. That was // survivable while every sheet passed through the active slot; it is not now // that a handed-over sheet deliberately never touches it. No sheet is a real // answer to "what is the active sheet" and no answer at all to "who owes what". if (!row) row = { store_id, published: 0, invoice: null, updated_at: null }; if (typeof row.invoice === 'string') { try { row.invoice = JSON.parse(row.invoice); } catch (e) {} } if (session.role !== 'admin' && row.invoice) row.invoice = stripCosts(row.invoice); // v93 — the oldest thing still waiting, so the screen can show the banner on // the same call it already makes. Best-effort: a missing table is simply no // handoff and must never stop the sheet loading. try { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const h = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice_number, started_at FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN} ORDER BY started_at ASC LIMIT 1`).bind(store_id).first().catch(() => null); const c = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN}`) .bind(store_id).first().catch(() => null); row.handoff = h || null; row.handoff_open_count = (c && c.n) || 0; // v107 — the WHOLE open list, minus the sheet blobs. The screen has to work // out which of these is waiting on the person now looking at it, and it // cannot do that from "the oldest one overall": with sheets moving in both // directions the oldest open row is regularly somebody else's. Names and // roles already live on the client, so the pick happens there rather than // teaching the Worker to guess at identity from a free-text name. const ol = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice_number, started_at FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN} ORDER BY started_at ASC LIMIT 50`).bind(store_id).all().catch(() => ({ results: [] })); row.handoff_open_list = (ol && ol.results) || []; } catch (e) { row.handoff = null; row.handoff_open_count = 0; row.handoff_open_list = []; } return json({ data: row }); } // v107 — a handoff is no longer one-directional, so "open" is no longer one // state. needs_pricing goes out to the person who names and prices; needs_review // and question come BACK to whoever sent it. All three mean somebody still owes // somebody something, and every query that used to test = 'needs_pricing' has to // test all three or a returned sheet is invisible — which is the whole point of // returning it. One constant, so a future state cannot be added to some queries // and forgotten in others. const MB_HANDOFF_OPEN = "('needs_pricing','needs_review','question')"; // ── v92 — THE HANDOFF ─────────────────────────────────────────────────── // Additive tables, created on first use and never migrated, so an older sheet // is unaffected and a failure here can only disable the handoff — never the // linesheet. async function mbEnsureHandoff(env) { try { await env.DB.prepare(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS handoff ( store_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, state TEXT, from_name TEXT, to_name TEXT, note TEXT, item_count INTEGER, started_at TEXT, finished_at TEXT)`).run(); } catch (e) { /* already there */ } try { await env.DB.prepare(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS push_subs ( endpoint TEXT PRIMARY KEY, who TEXT, created_at TEXT)`).run(); } catch (e) { /* already there */ } // v93 — one row per handoff, each carrying its OWN copy of the sheet. v92 // kept a single row and read the sheet out of active_invoice, so a second // handoff overwrote the first and the recipient was shown whatever happened // to be in the active slot. The snapshot is what makes them independent. try { await env.DB.prepare(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS handoff_queue ( id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, store_id TEXT, state TEXT, from_name TEXT, to_name TEXT, note TEXT, item_count INTEGER, style_count INTEGER, vendor TEXT, invoice TEXT, started_at TEXT, finished_at TEXT, finished_by TEXT)`).run(); } catch (e) { /* already there */ } try { await env.DB.prepare(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_handoff_open ON handoff_queue (store_id, state, started_at)`).run(); } catch (e) { /* already there */ } // v107 — added lazily, the same way every other column on this app has been // added, so a store already carrying handoff rows keeps them. The catch is // load-bearing: after the first run this ALTER always throws "duplicate // column name" and that is the success path, not a fault. try { await env.DB.prepare(`ALTER TABLE handoff_queue ADD COLUMN parent_id TEXT`).run(); } catch (e) { /* already there */ } // v110 — WHICH SHEET IS THIS. The queue stored the vendor and the whole // invoice blob but never the invoice NUMBER as a column, so nothing could // cheaply join a handoff to its Archive row: vendor alone is ambiguous the // moment a brand is bought twice. Added lazily like every other column here. let addedInvNo = false; try { await env.DB.prepare(`ALTER TABLE handoff_queue ADD COLUMN invoice_number TEXT`).run(); addedInvNo = true; // only a REAL add gets the backfill; the duplicate-column throw skips it } catch (e) { /* already there */ } if (addedInvNo) { // Every existing row already carries the answer inside its snapshot, so the // history is recoverable rather than starting blank from today. try { await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE handoff_queue SET invoice_number = json_extract(invoice, '$.invoice_number') WHERE invoice_number IS NULL AND invoice IS NOT NULL`).run(); } catch (e) { /* a store on an SQLite without json_extract simply starts from now */ } } } // The banner asks one question — is anything waiting — so this answers with the // OLDEST one still open. Oldest, not newest: the thing that has been waiting // longest is the thing most likely to have been forgotten. async function handoffStatus(env, store_id) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const row = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, started_at FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN} ORDER BY started_at ASC LIMIT 1`).bind(store_id).first().catch(() => null); const openCount = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN}`) .bind(store_id).first().catch(() => ({ n: 0 })); return json({ data: row || null, open_count: (openCount && openCount.n) || 0 }); } // The list, without the sheet blobs — a queue view should not drag every sheet // across the wire to render six lines of text. async function handoffList(env, store_id) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const open = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice_number, started_at FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN} ORDER BY started_at ASC`).bind(store_id).all().catch(() => ({ results: [] })); const recent = await env.DB.prepare( `SELECT id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice_number, started_at, finished_at, finished_by FROM handoff_queue WHERE store_id = ? AND state NOT IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN} ORDER BY finished_at DESC LIMIT 60`).bind(store_id).all().catch(() => ({ results: [] })); return json({ open: (open && open.results) || [], recent: (recent && recent.results) || [] }); } // One handoff, with its sheet. async function handoffOpen(request, env, store_id) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const id = String(b.id || ''); if (!id) return json({ error: 'Which handoff?' }, 400); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM handoff_queue WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(id, store_id).first().catch(() => null); if (!row) return json({ error: 'That handoff is no longer there — it may have been finished or cancelled.' }, 404); if (typeof row.invoice === 'string') { try { row.invoice = JSON.parse(row.invoice); } catch (e) { row.invoice = null; } } return json({ data: row }); } // Brian hands the sheet over. The notification is best-effort by design: the // state is what matters and it is already written, so a Twilio outage delays a // text, it does not lose the handoff. Which channels actually fired comes back // in the response so the screen can say so rather than implying a text was sent. async function handoffStart(request, env, store_id, session) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const note = String(b.note || '').slice(0, 300); const to = String(b.to || 'Kelly').slice(0, 60); const inv = b.invoice || null; const styles = (inv && Array.isArray(inv.styles)) ? inv.styles : []; if (!styles.length) return json({ error: 'That sheet has nothing on it to hand over.' }, 400); const count = parseInt(b.item_count, 10) || styles.reduce(function (t, st) { return t + (((st && st.colors) || []).length || 1); }, 0); const now = new Date().toISOString(); const from = (session && session.name) || 'the buyer'; const id = (crypto.randomUUID ? crypto.randomUUID() : ('h' + now + Math.floor(performance.now()))).slice(0, 40); // v107 — handing on a sheet that was itself handed to you closes the row it // arrived on. Without this a sheet Kelly sent back for review, which Brian // then sent straight out again, would sit in the queue as still waiting on // Brian forever, and the count on the banner would never come down. const fromHandoff = String(b.from_handoff || ''); // v93 — the SNAPSHOT is the point. A handoff that reads its sheet out of the // shared active slot is a handoff that changes under the person doing it. await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO handoff_queue (id, store_id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice, started_at, finished_at, finished_by, invoice_number) VALUES (?, ?, 'needs_pricing', ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, NULL, NULL, ?)` ).bind(id, store_id, from, to, note, count, styles.length, String((inv && inv.vendor) || ''), JSON.stringify(inv), now, String((inv && inv.invoice_number) || '')).run(); // v110 const msg = from + ' sent ' + (count || 'some') + ' item' + (count === 1 ? '' : 's') + ' to name and price' + (note ? ' — ' + note : '') + '.'; if (fromHandoff) { try { await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE handoff_queue SET state = 'handed_back', finished_at = ?, finished_by = ? WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ? AND state IN ${MB_HANDOFF_OPEN}` ).bind(now, from, fromHandoff, store_id).run(); } catch (e) { /* the new handoff is written and is what matters */ } } const delivery = await notifyStaff(env, msg); return json({ ok: true, id, state: 'needs_pricing', started_at: now, item_count: count, delivery }); } // Kelly is done. Notifies back, because the person waiting is the one who needs // to know — the same reason she needed telling in the first place. // Closes ONE handoff by id. v92 closed "the" handoff, which with a queue would // mean finishing one sheet silently cleared every other sheet waiting. async function handoffDone(request, env, store_id, session) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const id = String(b.id || ''); const state = (b.cancelled ? 'cancelled' : 'done'); const now = new Date().toISOString(); const who = (session && session.name) || 'someone'; if (!id) return json({ error: 'Which handoff?' }, 400); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT vendor, from_name, item_count FROM handoff_queue WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?') .bind(id, store_id).first().catch(() => null); if (!row) return json({ error: 'That handoff is no longer there.' }, 404); await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE handoff_queue SET state = ?, finished_at = ?, finished_by = ? WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?` ).bind(state, now, who, id, store_id).run(); // A cancellation is the sender changing their mind — nobody needs telling. const delivery = (state === 'done') ? await notifyStaff(env, who + ' finished naming and pricing the ' + (row.vendor || '') + ' sheet.') : { sms: 'not sent (cancelled)', push: 'not sent (cancelled)' }; return json({ ok: true, state, finished_at: now, delivery }); } // v107 — THE WAY BACK. // // Until now the handoff ran one way: Brian sent, Kelly priced, Kelly closed it. // "Closed" was the only thing she could say, so anything else — I have finished, // look at it before it goes live / I am stuck on this one — went back to being a // text message, which is the arrangement the handoff exists to replace. // // A return is a NEW row, not a flag on the old one. Two reasons. The sheet that // comes back is not the sheet that went out (it now has names and prices on it), // so it needs its own snapshot the same way the outbound one did. And the queue // is the record of who owed what to whom, which a mutated row would erase. // The original is closed as 'handed_back' and the new row points at it. // // mode 'review' → state 'needs_review' — finished, wants a look before it goes live // mode 'question' → state 'question' — not finished, blocked, needs an answer async function handoffReturn(request, env, store_id, session) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const id = String(b.id || ''); if (!id) return json({ error: 'Which handoff?' }, 400); const mode = (String(b.mode || 'review') === 'question') ? 'question' : 'review'; const state = (mode === 'question') ? 'question' : 'needs_review'; const note = String(b.note || '').slice(0, 300); const who = (session && session.name) || 'someone'; const now = new Date().toISOString(); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM handoff_queue WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(id, store_id).first().catch(() => null); if (!row) return json({ error: 'That handoff is no longer there — it may have been finished or cancelled.' }, 404); // Her edits, if the screen sent them. If it did not, the sheet that goes back // is the snapshot as it stands rather than nothing — a return that silently // drops an hour of pricing is worse than a return that is slightly stale. let inv = b.invoice || null; if (!inv) { inv = row.invoice; if (typeof inv === 'string') { try { inv = JSON.parse(inv); } catch (e) { inv = null; } } } const styles = (inv && Array.isArray(inv.styles)) ? inv.styles : []; const count = parseInt(b.item_count, 10) || styles.reduce(function (t, st) { return t + (((st && st.colors) || []).length || 1); }, 0) || (row.item_count || 0); // Addressed back to whoever sent it. from_name on the original is a real // person's name written by the Worker from their session, not typed, so this // cannot address a sheet to somebody who does not exist. const backTo = String(row.from_name || 'the buyer').slice(0, 60); const newId = (crypto.randomUUID ? crypto.randomUUID() : ('r' + now + Math.floor(performance.now()))).slice(0, 40); await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO handoff_queue (id, store_id, state, from_name, to_name, note, item_count, style_count, vendor, invoice, started_at, finished_at, finished_by, parent_id, invoice_number) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, NULL, NULL, ?, ?)` ).bind(newId, store_id, state, who, backTo, note, count, styles.length, String((inv && inv.vendor) || row.vendor || ''), JSON.stringify(inv), now, id, String((inv && inv.invoice_number) || row.invoice_number || '')).run(); // v110 // Only now is the original closed. If the insert above throws, the sheet is // still sitting in her queue rather than having vanished out of both. await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE handoff_queue SET state = 'handed_back', finished_at = ?, finished_by = ? WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?` ).bind(now, who, id, store_id).run(); const msg = (mode === 'question') ? who + ' has a question about the ' + (row.vendor || '') + ' sheet' + (note ? ' — ' + note : '') + '.' : who + ' finished the ' + (row.vendor || '') + ' sheet and sent it back for review' + (note ? ' — ' + note : '') + '.'; const delivery = await notifyStaff(env, msg); return json({ ok: true, id: newId, state, to_name: backTo, started_at: now, item_count: count, delivery }); } // v107 — autosave for a sheet that was OPENED FROM THE QUEUE. // // This is the fix for the collision, and it is worth stating plainly: there is // exactly ONE active_invoice row per store. Whoever saves last owns it. So while // Kelly priced a handed-over sheet on her phone and Brian worked a different // invoice on his computer, each autosave overwrote the other's — and the app // then loaded that shared row for both of them, which is why tapping a // notification landed on whatever invoice had been touched most recently rather // than on the sheet the notification was about. // // A sheet opened from the queue now saves into ITS OWN row and never touches the // active slot. Two people can work two sheets at once without meeting. async function handoffSave(request, env, store_id) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const id = String(b.id || ''); const inv = b.invoice || null; if (!id) return json({ error: 'Which handoff?' }, 400); if (!inv || !Array.isArray(inv.styles)) return json({ error: 'Nothing to save.' }, 400); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT state FROM handoff_queue WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(id, store_id).first().catch(() => null); if (!row) return json({ error: 'That handoff is no longer there.' }, 404); // A finished, cancelled or handed-back row is a RECORD. Writing to one would // rewrite history and, worse, would silently discard the edit — the person // typing would see "saved" over a row nobody will ever open again. if (['done', 'cancelled', 'handed_back'].indexOf(String(row.state)) >= 0) { return json({ error: 'That sheet has already been sent on — reopen it from the Handoffs tab.' }, 409); } const count = inv.styles.reduce(function (t, st) { return t + (((st && st.colors) || []).length || 1); }, 0); await env.DB.prepare( `UPDATE handoff_queue SET invoice = ?, item_count = ?, style_count = ?, vendor = ? WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?` ).bind(JSON.stringify(inv), count, inv.styles.length, String(inv.vendor || ''), id, store_id).run(); return json({ ok: true, id, item_count: count }); } // ── Notification ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── // Two channels, each independently optional. A channel that is NOT configured // reports itself as not configured — the failure mode of a notification system // is that it quietly stops and nobody finds out for a week. // v94 — opts.push=false suppresses web push for this call. Push here is // payload-free (RFC 8291 encryption was deliberately skipped), so the wording the // phone displays lives in sw.js and today it describes a LineSheet handoff. Any // caller whose message is NOT a handoff must opt out until sw.js can carry a // message, because a notification describing the wrong job is the lie that makes // the whole system untrustworthy. async function notifyStaff(env, text, opts) { const wantPush = !(opts && opts.push === false); const out = { sms: 'not configured', push: wantPush ? 'not configured' : 'suppressed for this message type' }; const url = (env.APP_URL || 'https://linesheet.theclearstack.com'); // ---- Twilio ---- if (env.TWILIO_SID && env.TWILIO_TOKEN && env.TWILIO_FROM && env.STAFF_PHONE) { try { const body = new URLSearchParams({ To: env.STAFF_PHONE, From: env.TWILIO_FROM, Body: text + ' ' + url }); const r = await fetch('https://api.twilio.com/2010-04-01/Accounts/' + env.TWILIO_SID + '/Messages.json', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Authorization': 'Basic ' + btoa(env.TWILIO_SID + ':' + env.TWILIO_TOKEN), 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' }, body: body.toString() }); const j = await r.json().catch(() => ({})); out.sms = r.ok ? 'sent' : ('failed: ' + (j.message || r.status)); } catch (e) { out.sms = 'failed: ' + ((e && e.message) || e); } } // ---- Web push ---- if (wantPush && env.VAPID_PUBLIC && env.VAPID_PRIVATE) { try { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const subs = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT endpoint FROM push_subs').all().catch(() => ({ results: [] })); const list = (subs && subs.results) || []; if (!list.length) { out.push = 'nobody has turned push on yet'; } else { let sent = 0, gone = 0, failed = 0; for (const row of list) { const res = await pushSend(env, row.endpoint); if (res === 'ok') sent++; else if (res === 'gone') { gone++; // 404/410 means the browser threw the subscription away. Deleting it // keeps the next send from spending time on a dead endpoint forever. try { await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM push_subs WHERE endpoint = ?').bind(row.endpoint).run(); } catch (e) {} } else failed++; } out.push = sent + ' sent' + (gone ? ', ' + gone + ' expired and removed' : '') + (failed ? ', ' + failed + ' failed' : ''); } } catch (e) { out.push = 'failed: ' + ((e && e.message) || e); } } return out; } // VAPID, payload-free. RFC 8291 payload encryption is a large amount of // hand-rolled ECDH and AES-GCM that cannot be verified without a live browser // subscription; a bare wake-up needs only the JWT, and the service worker // supplies the wording. Less to be silently wrong. function mbB64uToBytes(str) { const b = atob(String(str).replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/')); const out = new Uint8Array(b.length); for (let i = 0; i < b.length; i++) out[i] = b.charCodeAt(i); return out; } function mbBytesToB64u(bytes) { let s = ''; const a = new Uint8Array(bytes); for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) s += String.fromCharCode(a[i]); return btoa(s).replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, ''); } let _vapidKey = null; async function mbVapidKey(env) { if (_vapidKey) return _vapidKey; // The public key is the uncompressed point 0x04||x||y, so x and y come out of // it by slicing — one secret fewer to paste and one fewer to get wrong. const pub = mbB64uToBytes(env.VAPID_PUBLIC); if (pub.length !== 65 || pub[0] !== 4) throw new Error('VAPID_PUBLIC is not a 65-byte uncompressed P-256 point'); const jwk = { kty: 'EC', crv: 'P-256', ext: true, x: mbBytesToB64u(pub.slice(1, 33)), y: mbBytesToB64u(pub.slice(33)), d: String(env.VAPID_PRIVATE) }; _vapidKey = await crypto.subtle.importKey('jwk', jwk, { name: 'ECDSA', namedCurve: 'P-256' }, false, ['sign']); return _vapidKey; } async function pushSend(env, endpoint) { let aud; try { const u = new URL(endpoint); aud = u.protocol + '//' + u.host; } catch (e) { return 'gone'; } // an unparseable endpoint is never coming back const key = await mbVapidKey(env); const enc = new TextEncoder(); const header = mbBytesToB64u(enc.encode(JSON.stringify({ typ: 'JWT', alg: 'ES256' }))); const payload = mbBytesToB64u(enc.encode(JSON.stringify({ aud: aud, exp: Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000) + 12 * 3600, sub: env.VAPID_SUBJECT || 'mailto:brianburge@gmail.com' }))); const signing = header + '.' + payload; const sig = await crypto.subtle.sign({ name: 'ECDSA', hash: 'SHA-256' }, key, enc.encode(signing)); const jwt = signing + '.' + mbBytesToB64u(sig); let r; try { r = await fetch(endpoint, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Authorization': 'vapid t=' + jwt + ', k=' + env.VAPID_PUBLIC, 'TTL': '86400', 'Urgency': 'high', 'Content-Length': '0' } }); } catch (e) { return 'failed'; } if (r.status === 404 || r.status === 410) return 'gone'; return r.ok ? 'ok' : 'failed'; } // The browser hands us a subscription; we only ever need its endpoint, because // a payload-free push carries nothing to encrypt. Storing the keys we do not use // would be collecting something for no reason. async function pushSubscribe(request, env, session) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const endpoint = String((b.subscription && b.subscription.endpoint) || b.endpoint || ''); if (!/^https:\/\//.test(endpoint)) return json({ error: 'No push endpoint in that subscription.' }, 400); await env.DB.prepare( `INSERT INTO push_subs (endpoint, who, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?) ON CONFLICT(endpoint) DO UPDATE SET who = excluded.who` ).bind(endpoint, (session && session.name) || '', new Date().toISOString()).run(); return json({ ok: true }); } async function pushUnsubscribe(request, env) { await mbEnsureHandoff(env); const b = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const endpoint = String((b.subscription && b.subscription.endpoint) || b.endpoint || ''); if (endpoint) { try { await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM push_subs WHERE endpoint = ?').bind(endpoint).run(); } catch (e) {} } return json({ ok: true }); } // The public key is public by definition — the browser needs it to subscribe. async function pushKey(env) { return json({ key: env.VAPID_PUBLIC || '', configured: !!(env.VAPID_PUBLIC && env.VAPID_PRIVATE) }); } async function setActive(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); const existing = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM active_invoice WHERE store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id).first(); if (existing) { await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE active_invoice SET published = ?, invoice = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE store_id = ?') .bind(body.published ? 1 : 0, JSON.stringify(body.invoice), new Date().toISOString(), store_id).run(); } else { await env.DB.prepare('INSERT INTO active_invoice (store_id, published, invoice, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)') .bind(store_id, body.published ? 1 : 0, JSON.stringify(body.invoice), new Date().toISOString()).run(); } return json({ data: true }); } // v56 — the invoices table gained a `files` JSON column (scanned-page R2 keys). // D1 has no "ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS", so try once and swallow the duplicate- // column error. Cheap; runs at most a handful of times before it's a no-op. async function ensureInvoiceFilesColumn(env) { try { await env.DB.prepare('ALTER TABLE invoices ADD COLUMN files TEXT').run(); } catch (e) { /* already exists — ignore */ } } async function archiveInvoice(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); await ensureInvoiceFilesColumn(env); const filesJson = JSON.stringify(body.files || []); const hasFiles = (body.files || []).length > 0; // v99 — THE BUG: this lookup matched on invoice_number ALONE. Two DIFFERENT // vendors that both had a blank (or coincidentally identical) invoice number // were treated as "the same invoice" and their style arrays got merged // together into one archive row (reported: opening "A Shop of Things" from // Archive showed Valfre's products mixed in). The match now also requires // the vendor to agree (case/whitespace-insensitive) — body.vendor here is // always the TRUE original vendor (see sbArchiveInvoice on the client), // so this correctly still matches re-saves/republishes of the SAME invoice // while no longer colliding across different vendors. const vendorForMatch = String(body.vendor || '').trim(); const existing = await env.DB.prepare( 'SELECT * FROM invoices WHERE store_id = ? AND invoice_number = ? AND LOWER(TRIM(vendor)) = LOWER(?) LIMIT 1' ).bind(store_id, body.invoice_number || '', vendorForMatch).first(); if (existing) { // v34 MERGE — incoming styles WIN for matching codes (they carry the // latest names/prices; the old keep-existing rule froze the first // snapshot forever). Codes only present in the old record are kept. const existingStyles = JSON.parse(existing.styles || '[]'); const newStyles = body.styles || []; const merged = [...newStyles]; existingStyles.forEach(es => { if (!merged.find(ns => ns.code === es.code)) merged.push(es); }); // v56 — keep existing scanned files unless this save carries new ones. const filesToStore = hasFiles ? filesJson : (existing.files || filesJson); // v76 — the vendor was never refreshed on re-save, so a sheet archived // under the wrong name stayed wrong no matter how many times you saved it. const vendorToStore = String(body.vendor || '').trim() || existing.vendor || ''; await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE invoices SET vendor = ?, styles = ?, files = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?') .bind(vendorToStore, JSON.stringify(merged), filesToStore, new Date().toISOString(), existing.id).run(); } else { await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO invoices (store_id, vendor, invoice_number, invoice_date, status, published, styles, files, created_at, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)' ).bind(store_id, body.vendor || '', body.invoice_number || '', body.invoice_date || '', 'live', 1, JSON.stringify(body.styles), filesJson, new Date().toISOString(), new Date().toISOString()).run(); } return json({ data: true }); } // v76 — correct the vendor on an already-archived sheet. Needed because sheets // scanned before v76 had the private-label rewrite applied at extraction, so // the real vendor was never stored and cannot be recovered automatically. async function archiveSetVendor(request, env, store_id, session) { const b = await request.json(); const id = b.id; const vendor = String(b.vendor || '').trim(); if (!id) return json({ error: 'Missing invoice id' }, 400); if (!vendor) return json({ error: 'Vendor cannot be blank' }, 400); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT vendor FROM invoices WHERE store_id = ? AND id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id, id).first(); if (!row) return json({ error: 'That sheet is not in the archive' }, 404); await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE invoices SET vendor = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE store_id = ? AND id = ?') .bind(vendor, new Date().toISOString(), store_id, id).run(); try { await logChange(env, { user: (session && session.name) || 'app', action: 'archive_set_vendor', product_id: String(id), item_title: vendor, field: 'vendor', old_value: row.vendor || '', new_value: vendor }); } catch (e) {} return json({ ok: true, id: id, vendor: vendor, previous: row.vendor || '' }); } async function loadArchive(env, store_id) { await ensureInvoiceFilesColumn(env); const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM invoices WHERE store_id = ? ORDER BY created_at DESC') .bind(store_id).all(); return json({ data: rows.results || [] }); } async function deleteInvoice(url, env, store_id) { const id = url.searchParams.get('id'); // v56 — best-effort clean-up of the scanned files in R2 before the row goes. if (env.INVOICES) { try { const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT files FROM invoices WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?') .bind(id, store_id).first(); const files = row && row.files ? JSON.parse(row.files) : []; for (const f of files) { if (f && f.key) { try { await env.INVOICES.delete(f.key); } catch (e) {} } } } catch (e) { /* ignore — never block the delete */ } } await env.DB.prepare('DELETE FROM invoices WHERE id = ? AND store_id = ?').bind(id, store_id).run(); return json({ data: true }); } // ── v56: scanned invoice file storage (R2) ───────────────────────────── // store_invoice_file — body { image (base64, no data: prefix), media_type, // invoice_number, page, name }. Returns { key, type, name } to attach to the // invoice object. If R2 isn't bound yet, returns { skipped:true } so the app // degrades gracefully (extraction still works; there's just nothing to view). async function storeInvoiceFile(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); if (!env.INVOICES) return json({ skipped: true, reason: 'no_r2' }); const b64 = String(body.image || ''); if (!b64) return json({ error: 'Missing file data' }, 400); const mime = body.media_type || 'application/octet-stream'; const ext = mime === 'application/pdf' ? 'pdf' : (mime === 'image/png' ? 'png' : 'jpg'); // Sanitize the invoice number for the key; add a page index + short random. const invNum = String(body.invoice_number || 'noinv').replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9_\-]/g, '') || 'noinv'; const page = String(body.page == null ? 0 : body.page).replace(/[^0-9]/g, '') || '0'; const rand = Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8); const key = `invoices/${store_id}/${invNum}/${page}-${rand}.${ext}`; // base64 → bytes let bytes; try { const bin = atob(b64); bytes = new Uint8Array(bin.length); for (let i = 0; i < bin.length; i++) bytes[i] = bin.charCodeAt(i); } catch (e) { return json({ error: 'Bad file data' }, 400); } await env.INVOICES.put(key, bytes, { httpMetadata: { contentType: mime } }); return json({ key: key, type: mime, name: body.name || (invNum + '-' + page + '.' + ext) }); } // ── v108 — UPLOADED PRODUCT PHOTOS NOW SURVIVE A SAVE ─────────────────── // // THE BUG, reported 2026-08-14: "when i save progress the images are not saving // ... it even tells me the images are not there". It did, and it was telling the // truth. An uploaded photo lived as a data: URL for the thumbnail plus a Shopify // STAGED resourceUrl held in memory, and _spSnapshotCard deliberately stripped // every data: URL before saving — megabytes of base64 in the invoice row was the // right thing to refuse. But nothing durable was put in its place, so the photo // existed only until the page was closed. Saving the staged URL instead would // not have helped: a staged upload target is temporary by design. // // So the bytes now go into the R2 bucket the app already has bound (INVOICES), // under a photos/ prefix, and the card stores "r2:" — a short string that // is safe to persist, carries no session token, and can be resolved to a picture // by anyone who can already read the sheet. At push time the Worker reads the // object back out of R2 and stages it to Shopify itself (mbResolveImages), so // nothing about the push path changes from Shopify's point of view. // // Why not Shopify Files, which would give a permanent public URL and skip the // staging step: every photo uploaded for a product that never gets pushed would // sit in the live store's asset library forever with nothing linking it back, // and there is no safe automatic cleanup because a pushed product can reference // the same image. R2 keeps working material out of the store, and deleting an // archived invoice already deletes its R2 objects. const MB_R2_PREFIX = 'r2:'; function mbPhotoKey(store_id, invNum, code, ext) { const inv = String(invNum || 'noinv').replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9_\-]/g, '') || 'noinv'; const c = String(code || 'nocode').replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9_\-]/g, '') || 'nocode'; const rand = Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 10); return `photos/${store_id}/${inv}/${c}-${rand}.${ext}`; } // store_product_photo — body { image (base64, no data: prefix), media_type, // invoice_number, code, name }. Returns { ref } where ref is "r2:", which // is what the card stores. R2 unbound is reported plainly rather than silently // succeeding, because a photo that reports itself saved and is not is the exact // failure this whole change exists to end. async function storeProductPhoto(request, env, store_id) { if (!env.INVOICES) { return json({ error: 'Photo storage is not set up (no R2 bucket bound as INVOICES), so this photo could not be saved.' }, 503); } const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const b64 = String(body.image || ''); if (!b64) return json({ error: 'Missing image data' }, 400); const mime = String(body.media_type || 'image/jpeg'); if (!/^image\/(jpeg|png|webp|gif|avif)$/i.test(mime)) { return json({ error: 'Only JPEG, PNG, WEBP, GIF or AVIF images can be uploaded.' }, 400); } let bytes; try { const bin = atob(b64); bytes = new Uint8Array(bin.length); for (let i = 0; i < bin.length; i++) bytes[i] = bin.charCodeAt(i); } catch (e) { return json({ error: 'Image data was not valid base64' }, 400); } if (bytes.length > 20 * 1024 * 1024) return json({ error: 'Image is larger than 20MB' }, 400); const ext = /png/i.test(mime) ? 'png' : /webp/i.test(mime) ? 'webp' : /gif/i.test(mime) ? 'gif' : /avif/i.test(mime) ? 'avif' : 'jpg'; const key = mbPhotoKey(store_id, body.invoice_number, body.code, ext); await env.INVOICES.put(key, bytes, { httpMetadata: { contentType: mime } }); return json({ ok: true, ref: MB_R2_PREFIX + key, key: key, bytes: bytes.length }); } // v108 — turn any "r2:" entries in an images array into Shopify staged // resourceUrls. Everything else (a vendor URL, an already-staged resourceUrl) // passes through untouched. A photo that cannot be resolved is DROPPED and // named in the returned notes rather than sent to Shopify as a URL it cannot // fetch — a product that silently publishes with one photo missing is worse // than one that says which photo it lost. async function mbResolveImages(env, images) { const out = [], notes = []; for (const u of (images || [])) { const s = String(u || ''); if (s.indexOf(MB_R2_PREFIX) !== 0) { if (s) out.push(s); continue; } const key = s.slice(MB_R2_PREFIX.length); if (!env.INVOICES) { notes.push('A saved photo could not be attached: photo storage is not set up.'); continue; } try { const obj = await env.INVOICES.get(key); if (!obj) { notes.push('A saved photo is no longer in storage (' + key.split('/').pop() + ') and was not attached.'); continue; } const ct = (obj.httpMetadata && obj.httpMetadata.contentType) || 'image/jpeg'; const buf = new Uint8Array(await obj.arrayBuffer()); out.push(await mbStageBytes(env, buf, key.split('/').pop(), ct)); } catch (e) { notes.push('A saved photo could not be attached (' + key.split('/').pop() + '): ' + ((e && e.message) || e)); } } return { images: out, notes }; } // get_invoice_file — streams a stored scan back INLINE (viewable in a tab). // Opened as a plain link, so the session token arrives via ?token= (see // verifySession). ?key= is the R2 object key. Only serves keys under this // store's prefix, so one store can't read another's scans. async function getInvoiceFile(url, env, store_id, session) { if (!env.INVOICES) return new Response('Invoice storage is not set up yet.', { status: 503, headers: CORS }); const key = url.searchParams.get('key') || ''; // v108 — photos/ joins invoices/ here.
