Both are still scoped to THIS store's // prefix, so the rule that one store cannot read another's files is unchanged; // there is simply a second kind of file it may read of its own. const isPhoto = key && key.indexOf(`photos/${store_id}/`) === 0; const isInvoice = key && key.indexOf(`invoices/${store_id}/`) === 0; if (!isPhoto && !isInvoice) { return new Response('Invalid key', { status: 400, headers: CORS }); } // v109 — TWO KINDS OF FILE, TWO DIFFERENT BARS. A product photo is the work // itself and anyone who can price a sheet has to be able to see it. The // SCANNED INVOICE is not: it shows what we paid. The app tells staff on its // own screens that they see style names and retail prices only, so serving // the scan to a non-admin here would quietly break a promise the UI makes. if (isInvoice && !(session && session.role === 'admin')) { return new Response('Not permitted', { status: 403, headers: CORS }); } const obj = await env.INVOICES.get(key); if (!obj) return new Response('Not found', { status: 404, headers: CORS }); const ct = (obj.httpMetadata && obj.httpMetadata.contentType) || 'application/octet-stream'; return new Response(obj.body, { status: 200, headers: { ...CORS, 'Content-Type': ct, 'Content-Disposition': 'inline', 'Cache-Control': 'private, max-age=600' } }); } // Settings now hold ONLY non-secret app settings (location). API keys // and tokens live exclusively in Worker secrets and are never returned. async function getSettings(env, store_id) { const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT store_id, location_id, location_name, updated_at FROM settings WHERE store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id).first().catch(() => null); return json({ data: row || null }); } async function saveSettings(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); const existing = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT store_id FROM settings WHERE store_id = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id).first(); if (existing) { await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE settings SET location_id = ?, location_name = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE store_id = ?') .bind(body.location_id || null, body.location_name || null, new Date().toISOString(), store_id).run(); } else { await env.DB.prepare('INSERT INTO settings (store_id, location_id, location_name, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)') .bind(store_id, body.location_id || null, body.location_name || null, new Date().toISOString()).run(); } return json({ data: true }); } // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // CLAUDE PROXY — prompts moved verbatim from the old client code. // The browser never sees ANTHROPIC_KEY. // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ async function anthropic(env, body) { const res = await fetch('https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'x-api-key': env.ANTHROPIC_KEY, 'anthropic-version': '2023-06-01' }, body: JSON.stringify(body) }); const data = await res.json(); if (data.error) throw new Error('API: ' + data.error.message + ' (type: ' + data.error.type + ')'); if (!data.content || !data.content[0]) throw new Error('Empty response from Claude'); await logBurn(env, data.usage, body.model); return data; } // v103 — the FIRST TEXT block, not content[0]. Models with thinking enabled // (Sonnet 5, Opus 5 and Fable 5 have adaptive thinking always on) can return a // thinking block first, whose .text is undefined. Every caller that reads // content[0].text directly would get "undefined" and fail to parse. Falls back // to content[0] so behaviour is unchanged on models that never think. function firstText(data) { const blocks = (data && data.content) || []; for (const b of blocks) { if (b && b.type === 'text' && typeof b.text === 'string') return b.text; } return (blocks[0] && blocks[0].text) || ''; } // Invoice extraction — same model, same system prompt, same max_tokens // v75 — the private-label clause in the extraction prompt is generated from the // live list, so a vendor added in Settings is normalised at SCAN time too, not // only at push. Without this the app would show the real name on the sheet and // then silently swap it on push — the same see-one-thing-ship-another trap the // title bug came from. // v76 — DO NOT rewrite a private-label vendor here. // // v75 had extraction replace "Cotton Candy LA" with "Miss Behavin'" on the way // in. That destroyed the only record of who the goods were actually bought // from: by the time the app saw the invoice the real name was already gone, so // the ARCHIVE filed it under Miss Behavin' and the purchase history was lost. // (Visible in the archive as three "Cotton Candy LA" sheets from before the // rewrite sitting next to two "Miss Behavin'" ones from after it.) // // The customer-facing swap does NOT need to happen here. It is enforced // deterministically twice already: the app shows and sends Miss Behavin', and // createProduct scrubs every field on push. Extraction's job is to report what // the invoice SAYS. function _plPromptClause(names) { if (!names || !names.length) return ''; const q = names.map(n => '"' + n + '"').join(' / '); return "IMPORTANT - " + q + " are the store's OWN PRIVATE LABEL. Return the vendor EXACTLY as printed on the invoice for these; do NOT substitute another name. The app handles what customers see. "; } async function extractInvoice(request, env) { const _plNames = await privateLabelList(env); // v75 const { image, media_type } = await request.json(); if (!image || !media_type) return json({ error: 'Missing image' }, 400); // v37 — PDF invoices are first-class. The Claude API takes a whole PDF as a // "document" block (base64, application/pdf — no beta header; all active // models incl. Haiku read them; pages arrive as text + page images), so ONE // call reads every page of the invoice. Photos keep the image block path. const isPdf = media_type === 'application/pdf'; const contentBlock = isPdf ? { type: 'document', source: { type: 'base64', media_type: 'application/pdf', data: image } } : { type: 'image', source: { type: 'base64', media_type: media_type, data: image } }; // v103 (2026-08-11) — EXTRACTION NO LONGER NAMES ANYTHING. // Until v102 this one call was told to "generate a fun flirty 2-4 word name" // AND, ~900 characters later, to "copy that exact printed wording into // printed_name". A model primed to invent a name for an item does not then // transcribe that same item's name verbatim; the invention bleeds into the // transcription as added words and tidied spelling. That collision was // created in v75 (when printed_name was added on top of naming that had been // there since at least v9) and shipped unchanged through v102. // Extraction now reports ONLY what the invoice says. Naming moved to the // separate generate_name call the app already had, which never sees the // invoice and so cannot contaminate a transcription. const data = await anthropic(env, { // v103 — Sonnet 5, not Haiku 4.5. This is document transcription off a // photograph or PDF, which is where the cheapest tier is weakest, and the // models table lists PDF as a supported input for Sonnet 5 but not for // Haiku 4.5. ~9c per invoice against ~4.5c. Retyping 60% of a sheet costs // more than that. NOTE: this is the floating alias, so it tracks new // Sonnet 5 versions; pin it to a dated snapshot if that matters. model: 'claude-sonnet-5', max_tokens: isPdf ? 16000 : 4000, system: `You are a retail buying assistant for Miss Behavin', a women's boutique in Santa Barbara CA. Read this vendor invoice and report EXACTLY what it says. You are transcribing a document, not writing copy. Invent nothing. VENDOR: normalize to its canonical name, stripping legal suffixes like LLC, Inc, Corp, Ltd. Canonical vendors: Free People; Motel Rocks; Rolla's; Gorjana; Miss Behavin'; Lucy In The Sky; Matisse; Baggu; Emi Jay; Wildflower Cases; A Shop of Things; Show Me Your MUMU; Valfre; Boy's Lie. Always correct these forms: "Matise" -> Matisse; "Wild Flower" -> Wildflower Cases; "A Shop if Things" -> A Shop of Things; "BAGGU" -> Baggu; "LITS"/"lucy in the sky" -> Lucy In The Sky (capital In The, matches the existing catalog); "fp" -> Free People; "motel" -> Motel Rocks. If the vendor matches none of these, return the invoice vendor name cleaned of legal suffixes. ${_plPromptClause(_plNames)} PRODUCT NAMES. This is the rule that matters most, so read it twice. You do NOT name products. Naming happens in a separate step you are not part of. Your only job with a name is to transcribe one that is already printed. - "printed_name": the product or style name AS PRINTED, character for character. Do NOT correct spelling. Do NOT fix capitalization. Do NOT expand abbreviations. Do NOT add descriptive words. Do NOT tidy it up. If the invoice prints "Gallivantng Skrt", you return "Gallivantng Skrt" exactly. - NEVER put the style code in printed_name. - "name_read" says how well you read it and MUST be exactly one of: "clear" = a product name is printed and you read every character with confidence. printed_name holds that text. "unsure" = something appears to be printed there, but it is blurry, cut off, partly obscured, ambiguous, or you would have to infer any part of it. printed_name MUST be null. "absent" = the invoice prints no product name for this style, only a code or number. printed_name MUST be null. - "unsure" is the correct and expected answer for a poor scan. It is ALWAYS better than a guess. A person can retype a blank in seconds; finding and correcting a plausible wrong name costs far more. If you are weighing whether to guess, the answer is "unsure". - "name" is ALWAYS null. Never put anything in it. Return ONLY valid JSON, no markdown: {"vendor":"string","invoice_number":"string","invoice_date":"string","styles":[{"code":"string","type":"Dress|Top|Sweater|Bottom|Skirt|Shorts|Jumpsuit|Set|Swim|Outerwear|Activewear|Accessory|Bag|Jewelry|Shoe|Other","name":null,"printed_name":"string or null","name_read":"clear|unsure|absent","sku":null,"cost":0.00,"colors":[{"color":"string","sizes":{"XS":0,"S":0,"M":0,"L":0,"XL":0},"total":0}],"notes":null,"barcode":null,"fabric":null,"fit":null,"printed_desc":"string or null"}]} WHAT THE ITEM IS. Two fields, and they are the reason a swimsuit was being listed as sunglasses. - "printed_desc": the invoice's OWN description of the item, transcribed verbatim, e.g. "Swim Suit, Knit", "Woven Dress", "Denim Jacket". Vendors print this next to the style code or under it. Strip country-of-origin marks like "*CHINA" and leading asterisks, keep the rest exactly as printed. If the invoice describes the item nowhere, return null. NEVER infer it from the style code or from a photograph. - "type": pick the ONE value from the enum that printed_desc supports. "Swim Suit" -> "Swim". A two-piece or a set -> "Set". Choose "Other" ONLY when printed_desc is null AND nothing else on the line says what the item is. "Other" is not a neutral default: downstream it means "nobody knows what this is", so a garment binned there arrives on the shop floor unnamed. ONE STYLE PER PRINTED LINE ITEM. Return exactly one entry in "styles" for each line the invoice prints, in printed order, and use the FULL style code as printed on that line, including any colour suffix. Do NOT merge two lines because their codes share a prefix, and do NOT split one line into several. If the invoice prints "BK26110- BRN" and "BK26110- PURPLE" as two lines, that is TWO entries with codes "BK26110- BRN" and "BK26110- PURPLE", not one entry with two colours. If the invoice states a style count ("Total 3 Style(s)"), your "styles" array MUST have exactly that many entries. Reading the same invoice twice must give the same answer both times. SIZE KEYS MUST MATCH WHAT THE INVOICE ACTUALLY PRINTS. Use exactly ONE of these four runs per style, never a mix: ALPHA apparel -> "XS","S","M","L","XL" (include every size the invoice lists, INCLUDING XL); WAIST/denim sizing for jeans, skirts, skorts and shorts -> the printed waist numbers as STRING keys, e.g. {"24":1,"25":3,"26":2}, valid 24 to 31; SHOE sizing -> the printed shoe numbers as STRING keys, e.g. {"7":1,"8":2,"9":1}, valid 5 to 11; ONE-SIZE items (accessories, hair clips, bags, hats, anything unsized) -> {"OS": totalQty}. If a bottom is printed with waist numbers use the WAIST run, not XS-XL; do not translate between runs. A size not on the invoice can be 0 or omitted. OTHER FIELDS. If a vendor SKU, item number, or style number distinct from the barcode is printed, put it in "sku"; otherwise null, never invent it. If a UPC, EAN, GTIN or barcode is printed, put its digits in "barcode"; otherwise null, never invent one. If the invoice states a fabric or material (e.g. "100% cotton", "linen blend", "ribbed knit") put that exact wording in "fabric"; if it states a fit, rise or silhouette (e.g. "relaxed", "bodycon", "high rise", "oversized", "A-line") put it in "fit"; otherwise leave each null. Never invent fabric or fit. The same honesty rule applies to all of these: unreadable means null, never a guess. TYPE comes from the ACTUAL item, not the invoice column: an ashtray, lighter, keychain, hat, hair clip or phone case is Accessory; a purse, tote or clutch is Bag; a necklace, ring, bracelet or earrings is Jewelry; sandals, boots or sneakers is Shoe. Use "Other" ONLY when the item genuinely fits none of these, because it publishes a Shopify product type of "Other", which is wrong for anything shoppable.`, messages: [{ role: 'user', content: [ contentBlock, { type: 'text', text: isPdf ? 'Extract all styles from EVERY page of this invoice PDF and return one combined JSON only.' : 'Extract all styles from this invoice page and return JSON only.' } ] }] }); // ── v117 — ANNOTATE THE EXTRACTION WITH THE OCCASION PROPOSAL ─────────── // The chips are computed here, at extraction, from the vendor + category + // description the model just read, and ride back on each style so the review // card opens with them already ticked. Nothing is written to Shopify by this; // the person still approves before push. // // FAILS OPEN, DELIBERATELY. If the model returned anything this cannot parse, // the ORIGINAL text is returned unchanged and the cards simply open with no // chips ticked. An intake that took two minutes and cost real money must not // be lost because a keyword map threw. let _text = firstText(data); try { const _clean = String(_text || '').trim() .replace(/^```json[\s]*/i, '').replace(/```$/, '').trim(); const _parsed = JSON.parse(_clean); if (_parsed && Array.isArray(_parsed.styles)) { _parsed.styles.forEach(function (st) { try { Object.assign(st, mbProposeOccasion(st, _parsed.vendor)); } catch (e) { /* per style */ } }); _text = JSON.stringify(_parsed); } } catch (e) { /* unparseable — hand back exactly what the model said */ } return json({ text: _text }); } /* v117 — the same proposal, on demand, for every path that does not go through extractInvoice: Manual Entry, a sheet archived before this version shipped, and a handoff priced on a phone. One call takes the whole sheet rather than one call per card, so opening an eight-style sheet costs one request. Read-only. It writes nothing and touches no Shopify API. */ async function proposeOccasionAction(request) { let b = {}; try { b = await request.json(); } catch (e) {} const styles = Array.isArray(b.styles) ? b.styles : []; const vendor = b.vendor || ''; return json({ ok: true, enum: { occasion: MB_OCCASION_ENUM, aesthetic: MB_AESTHETIC_ENUM }, proposals: styles.map(function (st) { const p = mbProposeOccasion(st || {}, vendor); p.code = (st && st.code) || ''; return p; }) }); } // Name generation — returns MULTIPLE distinct girl-name + type options as a // JSON array, excluding any provided `used` names. A randomized starting-letter // nudge keeps the variety changing on every tap (fixes the "same names" repeat). // ── v111 — THE NAMER MAY NOT INVENT THE NOUN ──────────────────────────────── // Reported: a Sunday Brunch swimwear invoice came back as "Iris Scrunchie Set" // and "Rio Beach Tote" on one run, and "Iris Clutch", "Remy Scarf" and "Olive // Sunglasses" on the next. Three different product categories for the same // three bikinis, none of them swimwear. // // It was not the model being unreliable. It was being asked to do the // impossible. The extraction enum had no value for swimwear, so a swimsuit fell // to "Other"; this function then received `type: "Other"` and a system prompt // telling it every name is "a cool girl's FIRST name plus THE ITEM TYPE". Given // no noun it supplied one, and since the prompt also shuffles the alphabet for // variety, it supplied a different one each time. The randomness was ours. // // A name here is not decoration: "Olive Sunglasses" ends up as the Shopify // product title, so an invented noun is a fabricated FACT about the garment, // reaching the live catalogue. So the noun is now an INPUT. If the caller cannot // say what the item is, v121 returns BARE first names that claim nothing, and // blank for a person to fill in — which builds 102 and 126 already handle and // already say out loud. const MB_NOUN_BY_TYPE = { dress:'Dress', top:'Top', sweater:'Sweater', bottom:'Pants', skirt:'Skirt', shorts:'Shorts', /* v122 - THE APP'S OWN CATEGORY LABELS RESOLVE TOO. The app sends the live Category box as `type` (build 162), but this map only knew the singular extraction enum - so "Dresses" picked in the dropdown resolved to nothing and the names came back bare when the person had already said what the item is. Every label the Category dropdown offers now maps to its noun. */ dresses:'Dress', tops:'Top', blouses:'Top', 'tank tops':'Top', 'corset tops':'Top', bodysuits:'Bodysuit', sweaters:'Sweater', pants:'Pants', jeans:'Jeans', skirts:'Skirt', skorts:'Skort', 'rompers & jumpsuits':'Romper', sets:'Set', swimwear:'Swim', shoes:'Shoes', accessories:'Accessory', // v112 — 'Swim' is neutral: the enum says swim, it does not say which cut. jumpsuit:'Romper', set:'Set', swim:'Swim', outerwear:'Jacket', activewear:'Set', jewelry:'Jewelry', bag:'Bag', accessory:'Accessory', shoe:'Shoes' }; // The invoice's own words beat any mapping: "Swim Suit, Knit" is better evidence // than an enum value chosen by the same read. function mbNounFor(type, desc) { const d = String(desc || '').toLowerCase(); if (d) { // v112 — SWIM IS THREE ANSWERS, NOT TWO, AND THE DEFAULT MUST ASSERT NOTHING. // v111 returned "Swimsuit" whenever the invoice said swim without saying // set. That reads as ONE PIECE to everything downstream: the name became // "Frankie Swimsuit", the SEO writer described a "purple one piece swimsuit", // and the researched-vocabulary block handed it a stack of one-piece search // phrases to prefer. The item was a bikini. Nothing on the invoice said // one-piece — the word came from a default I chose. // "Swim Suit, Knit" states the CATEGORY, not the CUT. So an unstated cut now // gets the neutral noun "Swim" ("Frankie Swim"), which is ordinary boutique // wording and claims nothing. A cut is only named when the invoice names it. if (/\bbikini\b|\btwo[- ]?piece\b|\btankini\b/.test(d)) return 'Bikini Set'; if (/\bone[- ]?piece\b|\bmaillot\b/.test(d)) return 'One Piece'; if (/\bswim\b|\bswimsuit\b|\bswimwear\b/.test(d)) return /\bset\b|\bco-?ord\b/.test(d) ? 'Swim Set' : 'Swim'; if (/\bdress\b/.test(d)) return 'Dress'; if (/\bromper|jumpsuit\b/.test(d)) return /jumpsuit/.test(d) ? 'Jumpsuit' : 'Romper'; if (/\bskirt\b/.test(d)) return 'Skirt'; if (/\bshort\b/.test(d)) return 'Shorts'; if (/\bjean|denim\b/.test(d) && !/jacket/.test(d)) return 'Jeans'; if (/\bjacket|coat\b/.test(d)) return 'Jacket'; if (/\bsweater\b|\bcardigan\b|\bknitwear\b/.test(d)) return 'Sweater'; // NOT "knit top" — that is a Top if (/\bpant|trouser|legging\b/.test(d)) return 'Pants'; if (/\btop|blouse|tee|tank|cami\b/.test(d)) return 'Top'; if (/\bbag|tote|clutch|purse\b/.test(d)) return 'Bag'; if (/\bshoe|sandal|boot|heel|sneaker\b/.test(d)) return 'Shoes'; if (/\bset|co-?ord\b/.test(d)) return 'Set'; } const t = String(type || '').trim().toLowerCase(); if (t && t !== 'other' && t !== 'item') return MB_NOUN_BY_TYPE[t] || null; return null; } /* v119 — A NOUN THE PERSON TYPED IS ACCEPTED VERBATIM. v111 made the noun an INPUT and refused when it could not work one out, which was right and stays right: a noun the MODEL invents is a fabricated fact about the garment reaching the live catalogue, and that is how a bikini became "Olive Sunglasses". But mbNounFor has only two routes — regexes over the invoice wording, and a lookup of `type` against a closed 15-key map — so a real item the app simply has no word for (sarong, cover-up, sunglasses) could never be named at all, no matter what the person holding the invoice typed. App build 163 added a "what is it?" box for exactly this and had nowhere to send the answer; 165 routes it through `desc`, which works only for words the regexes already know. So the caller may now state the noun outright. This does NOT reopen v111: the model still may not choose the noun, it is handed one — the only change is that a HUMAN may be the one handing it over. Reading a fact off an invoice is not inventing one. Sanitised because it lands verbatim in a Shopify product title: one line, no control characters, no markup, nothing that would break a title or a filename, and capped. A word typed in lower case is title-cased so "bikini set" does not ship as "Amelia bikini set"; existing capitals are left alone so "Cover-Up" and "PJ Set" survive as typed. */ function mbTypedNoun(raw) { // Control characters become SPACES rather than vanishing. Deleting them fused // "Bikini\n\nSet" into "BikiniSet"; found in testing, not in production. // Stripped by code point, not by an escape range, so nothing here depends on // a backslash surviving a copy or a paste. let v = String(raw || '').split('') .map(c => c.charCodeAt(0) < 32 ? ' ' : c).join(''); // Whole tags go before the character filter. Removing only the angle brackets // turned "Dress" into "bDress/b", which would have shipped as a // product title. Also found in testing. v = v.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, ' ').replace(/[<>]/g, ' '); v = v.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim(); if (!v) return null; // Kept: letters, digits, space, apostrophe, ampersand, hyphen. No slash — it // is a path separator everywhere this title is later used as a filename. v = v.replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9 '&-]+/g, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim(); if (v.length < 2 || v.length > 30) return null; // not a noun; probably a sentence or a slip if (!/[A-Za-z]/.test(v)) return null; // digits alone name nothing return v.split(' ').map(w => (w && w === w.toLowerCase()) ? w.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + w.slice(1) : w).join(' '); } /* v120 - THE FIRST NAMES ARE A CURATED LIST NOW, NOT A MODEL'S TASTE. Asked for 2026-08-28: girls' first names in the 13-32 register, the sort that reads as current fashion and influencer naming, plus solidly popular American names. The old instruction was "an actual girl's name that feels fresh and a little unexpected, popular but not overused", which is vague enough to have produced Windy, Taffy and Octavia. WHY A LIST RATHER THAN ASKING A MODEL FOR CURRENT NAMES: the generate_name call has no web access. It is a plain API call, so it cannot look anything up and its idea of "current" is a training snapshot nobody can see or edit. A list is also a snapshot - but a snapshot you can read, edit and veto, which is the only difference that matters. Add and remove freely; nothing else in the Worker reads this array. And the model is gone from this path entirely. Its only job was to pick a first name and append a noun the caller already supplies, which is two lines of code. Picking here makes the result instant, free, and makes the duplicate guard EXACT rather than a politely worded request the model may ignore. */ const MB_NAME_POOL = [ 'Adeline','Alina','Amara','Amelie','Analia','Arden','Ariana','Aspen','Astrid','Aubrey', 'Aurora','Ayla','Bellamy','Birdie','Blair','Blake','Bonnie','Braelyn','Briar','Brielle', 'Brooke','Callie','Camila','Cara','Carys','Cassidy','Cecilia','Celine','Charlie','Clara', 'Colette','Cora','Daisy','Dakota','Dalia','Delaney','Demi','Eden','Elena','Eliza', 'Ellery','Elodie','Emberly','Emerson','Emilia','Esme','Estelle','Evangeline','Everly','Faye', 'Fern','Finley','Fiona','Florence','Frankie','Freya','Gemma','Georgia','Gia','Giselle', 'Goldie','Greer','Hadley','Halle','Harlow','Harper','Hazel','Imani','Indie','Iris', 'Isla','Ivy','Jada','Jade','Janelle','Jemma','Jolie','Jordyn','Josie','Juliet', 'Juno','Kai','Kaia','Kalani','Karina','Kehlani','Kenzie','Kiara','Lainey','Lana', 'Lark','Layla','Leighton','Lena','Lennon','Liv','Lola','Londyn','Lucia','Luna', 'Maeve','Magnolia','Maia','Mara','Marlowe','Maya','Mila','Milan','Mira','Monroe', 'Nadia','Naomi','Nell','Nia','Noa','Nola','Nova','Oakley','Olive','Ophelia', 'Paloma','Paris','Parker','Paulina','Pearl','Penelope','Phoebe','Piper','Presley','Priya', 'Quinn','Raegan','Rae','Reese','Remi','Rhea','Riley','Rio','Rome','Romy', 'Rosalie','Rowan','Ruby','Sable','Sadie','Sage','Saoirse','Sasha','Savannah','Scarlett', 'Selena','Serena','Shiloh','Sienna','Simone','Skye','Sloane','Sofia','Soleil','Stella', 'Sunny','Sutton','Sydney','Tallulah','Tatum','Teagan','Thea','Tessa','Valentina','Vada', 'Vera','Veda','Violet','Wren','Willa','Winnie','Yara','Zaria','Zoey','Zuri' ]; /* The first-name part of anything already used, so "Sienna Dress" blocks "Sienna Top" as well. The caller sends live Shopify titles, every name already shown for this style, and every name on another card in the same push, and all three arrive in `used` - see _spNamesInThisPush and _shopifyExistingNames in the app. Matching on the first WORD is what makes one style code unable to collide with another across a push. */ function mbUsedFirstNames(used) { const set = new Set(); for (const u of (used || [])) { const first = String(u || '').trim().split(/\s+/)[0]; if (first) set.add(first.toLowerCase()); } return set; } async function generateName(request, env) { const body = await request.json(); const type = body.type || 'Item'; const desc = body.desc || body.printed_desc || ''; // v119 — a noun stated by the caller wins over anything inferred. App build // 165 already sends this field; older apps do not and fall through unchanged. const noun = mbTypedNoun(body.noun) || mbNounFor(type, desc); /* v121 - NO MORE REFUSAL. Decided by Brian 2026-08-28, said three times before it was heard: the refresh button must return name choices REGARDLESS, exactly as it does when the invoice printed a name. v111's rule was that an unknown item must not be given an invented noun, because a noun is a claim about the garment and "Olive Sunglasses" on a bikini is a false one. That rule is KEPT - not by asking first, but by asserting nothing: when the noun is unknown the names come back BARE. "Sienna", "Marlowe", "Paloma" - the same way the store already names its boots (Monarch, Bodhi, Dixie, Patsy). A bare first name claims nothing about the garment, so there is nothing to be wrong about, and the person picking it sees exactly what the title will be. Typing the item in the box still works and still upgrades the names to "Sienna Bikini Set" - it is now a refinement, never a gate. */ const used = Array.isArray(body.used) ? body.used : []; const count = Math.min(Math.max(parseInt(body.count, 10) || 5, 1), 8); /* v120 - PICKED FROM MB_NAME_POOL IN CODE. No model call on this path. The exclusion is now EXACT rather than a request in a prompt: a first name already live in Shopify, already offered for this style, or already taken by another card in this same push cannot come back, because it is filtered out before anything is chosen rather than asked-not-to-be-used afterwards. Shuffled per call so More returns a genuinely different set. */ const taken = mbUsedFirstNames(used); const free = MB_NAME_POOL.filter(n => !taken.has(n.toLowerCase())); for (let i = free.length - 1; i > 0; i--) { const j = Math.floor(Math.random() * (i + 1)); const t = free[i]; free[i] = free[j]; free[j] = t; } const picked = free.slice(0, count); if (!picked.length) { // Said out loud rather than returning an empty list that reads as a failure. return json({ names: [], text: '', pool_exhausted: true, reason: 'Every name in the list is already used by a live product or by another card in this push. Type a name yourself, or add more names to MB_NAME_POOL in the Worker.' }); } return json({ names: picked.map(n => noun ? (n + ' ' + noun) : n), noun: noun || '', source: 'pool' }); } // ── SEO + on-page copy + category (v2) ───────────────────────── // v30 — the product copy rules live in ONE constant, shared by the intake // path (generateSeo) and the manual-add fix path (seoProductFix). const SEO_PRODUCT_SYSTEM = `You write ecommerce listing copy for Miss Behavin', a women's boutique selling nationally online. Using ONLY the product facts provided, return STRICT JSON (no markdown, no commentary) with exactly these keys: seo_title, seo_description, image_alt, body_description, product_category, tags. Invent nothing beyond the facts given. NEVER STATE A CUT OR CONSTRUCTION THE FACTS DO NOT STATE. This is the one invention that keeps happening and it is the most damaging, because it describes the wrong garment to a shopper who is about to buy it. Words like one piece, two piece, bikini, tankini, monokini, high waisted, low rise, cheeky, thong, halter, strapless, bandeau, cropped, lined, padded, underwire, sleeveless, long sleeve, mini, midi and maxi are CLAIMS about the item. Use one ONLY if it appears in the style name, the garment type, the fit field or the fabric field you were given. If the facts say "Swim" they tell you the CATEGORY, not the CUT: write "swimwear" or "swim style" and say nothing about how many pieces it has. A correct, slightly vaguer sentence beats a specific wrong one every time — the shopper can see the photograph, and a description that contradicts it costs a return and the trust behind it. Brand normalization: map the given brand to its canonical name before using it — "fp"/"free people" -> Free People; "motel"/"motel rocks" -> Motel Rocks; "rollas"/"rolla's" -> Rolla's; "gorjana" -> Gorjana; "cotton candy"/"cotton candy la" -> Miss Behavin' (Cotton Candy is the store's own PRIVATE LABEL — treat it as a house item and NEVER output the words "Cotton Candy" in any field); "lucy in the sky"/"lits" -> Lucy In The Sky; "matisse"/"matise"/"matisse footwear" -> Matisse; "baggu"/"BAGGU" -> Baggu; "emi jay"/"emijay" -> Emi Jay; "wildflower"/"wild flower"/"wildflower cases" -> Wildflower Cases; "a shop of things"/"a shop if things"/"asot" -> A Shop of Things; "mumu"/"show me your mumu" -> Show Me Your MUMU; "valfre" -> Valfre; "boys lie"/"boy's lie" -> Boy's Lie. A style is a "named label" when it carries a real external brand; it is a "house item" when the brand is Miss Behavin', blank, or "(house label / none)". seo_title: "Style Name | Brand | Miss Behavin'" when a named label; "Style Name | Miss Behavin'" for house items. LENGTH IS ENFORCED BY THE VALIDATOR, NOT BY YOU — aim for the ideal band and never pad to reach a minimum. If it runs long, shorten the Style Name portion only; never truncate mid-word. No double quotes. seo_description (LENGTH IS ENFORCED BY THE VALIDATOR against missb_metadata_RULES_v1.json — never pad with filler to reach any number; put the distinguishing detail FIRST because front-loading is what does the work): lead with WHAT THE PIECE IS, name the brand, and pack in as many CONCRETE, searchable details as the data provides — fabric, fit/silhouette, color, and the garment's use or occasion. Do NOT add shipping, returns, or "in stock" boilerplate: availability travels through structured data and the feed, and repeating the same closing phrase on every product is duplicate boilerplate that weakens the page. Every description should read differently because its details differ. Written for a national shopper. No city name, no store/physical-store mention. NAME THE STYLE ONCE. The style name you were given must appear in this sentence, spelled exactly as given. Put it EARLY but never at the cost of the concrete details, because the details are what a shopper actually searches: "Testing mint mini dress from Miss Behavin', ribbed knit with a fitted waist" is right; a sentence that spends its opening clause on the name alone is not. Never invent, shorten or re-capitalize the name, and never say it twice. image_alt (max 110 characters): brand (if named), style, color, garment type, ending "at Miss Behavin'". body_description (2-4 short sentences, about 30-60 words): a punchy, boutique-voice description a shopper reads on the product page. Lead with the vibe/what it is, name the brand if named, weave in the concrete details given. Warm and confident, not salesy. Never dump raw spec text like "SHELL: 100% POLYESTER" into a sentence. NAME THE STYLE ONCE, in the FIRST sentence, exactly as given \u2014 this is the name on the tag, on the sheet and on the shop floor, so the page should use it out loud ("The Testing dress is..."). Never repeat it in a later sentence, and never alter its spelling or capitalization. product_category: choose exactly ONE from this list of 16 — Dresses, Tops, Sweaters, Pants, Jeans, Skirts, Shorts, Rompers & Jumpsuits, Sets & Co-ords, Outerwear, Swimwear, Activewear, Jewelry, Bags, Accessories, Shoes. Use Pants for trousers, leggings, or any non-denim bottom; Jeans for denim; never "Bottoms", never "Intimates & Loungewear" (both retired). If nothing fits, use "Accessories". tags: an array of 4 to 8 short, shopper-searchable tags for Shopify filtering and search. Include: the exact style name as ONE tag (e.g. "Butter Me Up"); any garment FEATURE words that appear in the style name or the provided facts or (if a photo is attached) are clearly visible (e.g. lace, ruffle, cutout, bodycon, boy short, cropped, tie front, square neck); Do NOT include the color, the brand, sizes, or a guessed fabric — those are added automatically elsewhere. DO NOT WRITE AN OCCASION OR A VIBE. Not "going out", not "everyday", not "date night", not "vacation", not "festival", and no phrasing of the same idea. Occasion is a CLOSED, VALIDATED field the owner ticks on the review card, and it writes hyphenated values a Shopify collection rule reads exactly. A free-text occasion word from you lands beside that value as a near-duplicate no collection matches, which is the defect this instruction was changed to close (master item 93 — 68 products, uncontrolled vocabulary, averaging 6.5 tags each). Describe the GARMENT. Somebody else decides what it is worn to. Never invent a material or a feature you cannot see or read in the facts. Each tag is a short human phrase, not a sentence. CRITICAL — do NOT state or imply any fabric, material, fit, rise, cut, drape, weight, or silhouette unless it is EXPLICITLY present in the provided facts (a "Fabric:" or "Fit / silhouette:" line). If those are not provided, write only about the color and how to style or wear the piece; never guess descriptors like "relaxed fit", "fitted", "flowy", "structured", "clean lines", "lightweight", or "versatile silhouette". Styling suggestions (layer it, dress it up or down, pair it with) are allowed because they are advice, not claims about the garment. Writing rules (this copy is published): never use the word "literally"; NEVER use an em dash (\u2014) in seo_description or body_description \u2014 use a comma, a period, or "and" instead (it is allowed in seo_title); plain, confident language, lead with the point; no fabricated metrics, measurements, or details not in the data.`; // Appended to SEO_PRODUCT_SYSTEM ONLY when the product's photo is attached to // the call. It grants a narrow carve-out from the base no-guess rule: the model // may describe what it can actually SEE, because a photo of the exact product // makes visible structure a fact, not a guess. Fabric/fiber stay forbidden — a // photo cannot tell cotton from poly. This appears AFTER the base prompt so its // image_alt format and its override of the CRITICAL rule take precedence. const SEO_PRODUCT_VISION_CARVEOUT = ` ── PHOTO ATTACHED — visible structure now counts as fact (overrides the CRITICAL no-guess rule for the fields named below) ── A photo of this exact product is attached. For seo_description, image_alt, and body_description, treat structural details you can clearly SEE as provided facts: neckline, silhouette and cut, fit as shown, sleeve type and length, hemline and length (mini/midi/maxi), straps, ties, cutouts, ruffles, lace, buttons, collar, and pattern. Describe ONLY what is clearly visible; if a detail is not clearly visible, do not name it. Do not restate or re-guess the color — trust the provided Color value. STILL FORBIDDEN even with the photo: fabric, fiber, and material (a photo cannot confirm cotton vs. polyester vs. silk). Never state these unless a "Fabric:" fact is provided. image_alt (max 110 characters) — this REPLACES the base image_alt format: "Brand Style in color with <1 to 2 distinctive visible features>", where Brand is the named label, or "Miss Behavin'" for house items. Do NOT append "at Miss Behavin'" when features are present — the distinctive detail is the ranking value, not a repeated brand tag. Natural reading, no keyword stuffing, no comma-separated keyword lists. Example (house item): "Miss Behavin' Ophelia Top in coral with fitted corset bodice and open back". Example (named label): "Free People Adella Slip in sage with lace trim and adjustable straps".`; // v46 — appended when MULTIPLE photos of one product are attached. Redefines the // output to a product-level block plus a per-image array, so every photo gets a // unique alt describing THAT shot and a view label that becomes its filename. const SEO_PRODUCT_MULTI_CARVEOUT = ` ── MULTIPLE PHOTOS ATTACHED — one product, several views ── You are given the photos of ONE product, in gallery order, each preceded by "Image K:". Visible structure you can clearly SEE (neckline, silhouette, cut, fit as shown, sleeves, hemline and length, straps, ties, cutouts, ruffles, lace, buttons, collar, pattern) counts as fact for seo_description, body_description, and every image alt. This OVERRIDES the CRITICAL no-guess rule for those fields. STILL FORBIDDEN: fabric, fiber, and material (a photo cannot confirm them) unless a "Fabric:" fact is provided. Do not re-guess the color — trust the provided Color value. GARMENT NOUN LOCK: whenever an alt or the description names the garment, use ONLY the singular form of the provided "Garment category" (Dresses->dress, Skirts->skirt, Shorts->shorts, Tops->top, Sweaters->sweater, "Rompers & Jumpsuits"->romper, "Sets & Co-ords"->set, Outerwear->jacket or coat as seen). NEVER substitute a different garment word from the photo (do not turn a skirt into a romper or a dress into a skort). If the category is "(none set)" or plainly contradicts every photo, use the neutral word "piece". The photo decides the DETAILS, never the garment noun. IGNORE the single "image_alt" key from the base instructions. Return STRICT JSON (no markdown, no commentary) with EXACTLY these keys: { "seo_title": "product-level, base rule", "seo_description": "product-level, base rule, using what the photos show", "body_description": "product-level, base rule", "product_category": "one of the 16", "tags": [ "style name", "visible feature words like lace/ruffle/cutout", "a second visible feature word if there is one" ], "images": [ { "view": "...", "alt": "..." } ] } The "images" array has ONE entry per attached photo, IN THE SAME ORDER as attached (entry 1 = Image 1). "view" = choose the SINGLE best-fitting label from EXACTLY this list, nothing else: front, side, back, on-model, detail. Use on-model for any full-length or styled shot on a person, and detail for a close-up of a trim, fabric, or specific feature. Give different photos different labels where they differ; if two genuinely match the same label, reuse it (the app appends -2). Each "alt" (max 110 chars): "Brand Style in color with ", Brand = the named label or "Miss Behavin'" for house items; do NOT append "at Miss Behavin'". Lead the front alt with the neckline or silhouette, the back alt with the back, a detail alt with the specific detail. Every alt must be DIFFERENT and describe THAT photo. Natural reading, no keyword stuffing. Return the JSON now, nothing else.`; // Generated from the FINAL product data at review time. The browser calls // this, prefills EDITABLE fields, and the user's (possibly edited) values are // what get submitted to createProduct. The national copy formula is embedded // verbatim below. Uses ONLY the facts passed in — invents nothing. async function generateSeo(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); const { title, brand, type, colors, fabric, fit, price } = body; if (!title) return json({ error: 'Missing title' }, 400); // v83 — look up the researched phrases for this garment category and this // brand and hand them to the writer. Empty result is normal and reported, // never silently skipped. const _terms = await mbTermsForProduct(env, store_id || MB_STORE_ID_DEFAULT, { category: body.category || '', type: type || '', brand: brand || '', title: title || '' }); const colorStr = Array.isArray(colors) ? colors.filter(Boolean).join(', ') : (colors || ''); const facts = 'Style name: ' + title + '\nBrand: ' + (brand || '(house label / none)') + '\nGarment type: ' + (type || '') + '\nColor(s): ' + colorStr + (fabric ? '\nFabric: ' + fabric : '') + (fit ? '\nFit / silhouette: ' + fit : '') + (price ? '\nPrice: $' + price : ''); const data = await anthropic(env, { model: 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001', max_tokens: 500, system: SEO_PRODUCT_SYSTEM, messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'Product data:\n' + facts + mbTermsPromptBlock(_terms) + '\n\nReturn the JSON now.' }] }); // `registry` travels back so the Review screen can show which researched // words fed the copy — or say plainly that the category has none yet. return json({ text: data.content[0].text, registry: _terms }); } // ── Vendor product details (v2) ──────────────────────────────── // Pull images + fabric + fit from a vendor's OWN site by STYLE CODE, without // hand-written per-vendor scrapers. Most boutique brands run on Shopify, and // every Shopify store exposes a public JSON feed: // /products.json?limit=250&page=N -> full catalog (title, body_html, // variants[].sku, images[].src). We page through it and match the invoice // style code against the body "Style number:" line AND the variant SKUs // (verified: the style code lives in the SKU, e.g. CD-14328-RED-1, not in // the store's quick-search index — so we match locally, not via search). // /products/.json -> one product's images + description. // Fabric/fit are read from the description by Claude, so each vendor's wording // just works. Faire brands should use the official Faire Shopify app instead; // this is the fallback for direct-buy brands. // // Two modes on ONE action (the review screen calls search, staff pick, detail): // { vendor, styleCode } -> SEARCH: returns candidates[] (handle+title+colors+images) // { vendor, handle } -> DETAIL: returns { images, fabric, fit, description } // { productUrl } -> GENERIC fallback for a non-Shopify product page // // Per-vendor config is ONE line: the domain. Add brands as you carry them. const VENDOR_SITES = { 'cotton-candy-la': { domain: 'shop-cottoncandyla.com', platform: 'shopify' }, 'matisse': { domain: 'matissefootwear.com', platform: 'shopify' }, // CONFIRMED Shopify 7/14 (products.json returns catalog) 'emi-jay': { domain: 'emijay.com', platform: 'shopify' }, // CONFIRMED Shopify 7/14 (robots.txt signature) 'wildflower-cases': { domain: 'wildflowercases.com', platform: 'shopify' }, // CONFIRMED Shopify 7/14 (robots.txt signature) 'a-shop-of-things': { domain: 'ashopofthings.com', platform: 'shopify' }, // CONFIRMED Shopify 7/14 (robots.txt signature) 'baggu': { domain: 'baggu.com', platform: 'shopify' } // [VERIFY at first invoice] probe blocked 7/14; fetch fails gracefully if wrong // Lucy in the Sky EXCLUDED on purpose: lucyinthesky.com is NOT Shopify (OpenCart-style routes) — use the productUrl fallback for LITS images. // 'brand-key': { domain: 'brand.com', platform: 'shopify' }, }; const VENDOR_FETCH_UA = 'MissBehavinIntake/1.0 (+https://www.shopmissbehavin.com)'; // v57 — in-app vendor-site registry (D1). Staff add a brand's website once and // every future scanned product from that brand pulls images/fabric from it, // with no code edit. Keyed by the same vendorKey() the built-in list uses. async function ensureVendorSitesTable(env) { try { await env.DB.prepare( 'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS vendor_sites (store_id TEXT, vendor_key TEXT, vendor_name TEXT, domain TEXT, platform TEXT, updated_at TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (store_id, vendor_key))' ).run(); } catch (e) { /* best-effort */ } } function cleanDomain(d) { return String(d || '').trim().replace(/^https?:\/\//i, '').replace(/\/.*$/, '').replace(/^www\./i, '').toLowerCase(); } async function getVendorSite(env, store_id, vendor) { await ensureVendorSitesTable(env); const row = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT domain, platform FROM vendor_sites WHERE store_id = ? AND vendor_key = ? LIMIT 1') .bind(store_id, vendorKey(vendor)).first(); return row && row.domain ? { domain: row.domain, platform: row.platform || 'shopify' } : null; } async function saveVendorSite(request, env, store_id) { const body = await request.json(); const vendor = String(body.vendor || '').trim(); const domain = cleanDomain(body.domain); const platform = String(body.platform || 'shopify').trim() || 'shopify'; if (!vendor) return json({ error: 'Missing vendor' }, 400); if (!domain || domain.indexOf('.') < 0) return json({ error: 'Enter a valid website (e.g. brand.com)' }, 400); await ensureVendorSitesTable(env); await env.DB.prepare( 'INSERT INTO vendor_sites (store_id, vendor_key, vendor_name, domain, platform, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?) ' + 'ON CONFLICT(store_id, vendor_key) DO UPDATE SET vendor_name = excluded.vendor_name, domain = excluded.domain, platform = excluded.platform, updated_at = excluded.updated_at' ).bind(store_id, vendorKey(vendor), vendor, domain, platform, new Date().toISOString()).run(); return json({ ok: true, vendor: vendor, domain: domain, platform: platform }); } async function listVendorSites(env, store_id) { await ensureVendorSitesTable(env); const rows = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT vendor_key, vendor_name, domain, platform, updated_at FROM vendor_sites WHERE store_id = ? ORDER BY vendor_name').bind(store_id).all(); return json({ sites: (rows.results || []) }); } const VENDOR_MAX_PAGES = 6; // /products.json pages scanned before giving up function vendorKey(vendor) { return String(vendor || '').toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/^-|-$/g, ''); } function normCode(s) { return String(s || '').toUpperCase().replace(/\s+/g, '').trim(); } function bodyStyleNumber(body) { const m = /style\s*number\s*[:#]?\s*([A-Za-z0-9\-]+)/i.exec(body || ''); return m ? normCode(m[1]) : null; } function skuStyleCode(sku) { // 'CD-14328-RED-1' -> 'CD-14328'; 'CP-14274-1-WHITE-1' -> 'CP-14274-1' const parts = String(sku || '').split('-'); if (parts.length >= 3) return normCode(parts.slice(0, parts.length - 2).join('-')); return normCode(sku); } function productMatchesCode(product, code) { if (bodyStyleNumber(product.body_html) === code) return true; return (product.variants || []).some(function (v) { return skuStyleCode(v.sku) === code; }); } function stripHtml(html) { return String(html || '').replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ').replace(/&[a-z]+;/gi, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim(); } function candidateFrom(product) { const colors = (product.options || []) .filter(function (o) { return /colou?r/i.test(o.name); }) .reduce(function (a, o) { return a.concat(o.values || []); }, []); return { handle: product.handle, title: product.title, colors: colors, price: product.variants && product.variants[0] ? product.variants[0].price : null, images: (product.images || []).map(function (im) { return im.src; }), styleNumber: bodyStyleNumber(product.body_html) }; } // Claude pulls fabric + fit from arbitrary vendor description text — this is // what makes new vendors "just work" without a custom parser. async function extractFabricFit(env, descText) { if (!descText) return { fabric: null, fit: null }; try { const data = await anthropic(env, { model: 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001', max_tokens: 150, system: 'From the product description text, extract ONLY facts explicitly stated. Return STRICT JSON: {"fabric": string|null, "fit": string|null}. "fabric" = material/content (e.g. "97% cotton, 3% spandex"). "fit" = fit, rise, or silhouette in a few words (e.g. "fitted bodice, A-line, low-rise"). If a value is not stated, use null. Invent nothing. No markdown.', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: String(descText).slice(0, 4000) }] }); const j = JSON.parse(jsonSlice(data.content[0].text)); return { fabric: j.fabric || null, fit: j.fit || null }; } catch (e) { return { fabric: null, fit: null }; } } // Claude sometimes wraps JSON in ```json fences or adds a stray sentence. // Pull out the first {...} object so JSON.parse succeeds either way. function jsonSlice(text) { let t = String(text || '').trim(); t = t.replace(/^```(?:json)?\s*/i, '').replace(/```\s*$/i, '').trim(); const start = t.indexOf('{'), end = t.lastIndexOf('}'); return (start >= 0 && end > start) ? t.slice(start, end + 1) : t; } // Page a Shopify store's public products.json and collect products whose style // code matches (via body "Style number" or variant SKU). async function shopifyFindByCode(domain, code) { const matches = []; for (let page = 1; page <= VENDOR_MAX_PAGES; page++) { let res; try { res = await fetch('https://' + domain + '/products.json?limit=250&page=' + page, { headers: { 'User-Agent': VENDOR_FETCH_UA } }); } catch (e) { break; } if (!res.ok) break; const data = await res.json().catch(function () { return null; }); const products = (data && data.products) || []; if (!products.length) break; products.forEach(function (p) { if (productMatchesCode(p, code)) matches.push(p); }); if (products.length < 250) break; // reached the last page if (matches.length) break; // found on this page; color variants sit together } return matches; } // v58 — filter a Shopify product's images down to ONE colorway. A vendor // product bundles every color's photos (a 5-color bodysuit = ~40 images); when // staff are adding the "Black" one they only want Black's shots. Matches the // requested color against the product's color option, collects that colorway's // variant ids, and keeps only images tagged to those variants PLUS images tied // to no variant (gallery/flatlay shots that apply to all). If nothing matches, // returns ALL images so we never show an empty picker. function filterImagesByColor(product, wantColor) { const all = (product.images || []).map(function (im) { return im.src; }); const cw = String(wantColor || '').trim().toLowerCase(); if (!cw) return all; const opts = product.options || []; let colorIdx = -1; for (let i = 0; i < opts.length; i++) { if (/colou?r/i.test(opts[i].name || '')) { colorIdx = i; break; } } if (colorIdx < 0) return all; const optKey = 'option' + (colorIdx + 1); const colorVariantIds = (product.variants || []) .filter(function (v) { const val = String(v[optKey] || '').trim().toLowerCase(); return val && (val === cw || val.indexOf(cw) >= 0 || cw.indexOf(val) >= 0); }) .map(function (v) { return v.id; }); if (!colorVariantIds.length) return all; const idset = {}; colorVariantIds.forEach(function (id) { idset[id] = true; }); const kept = (product.images || []).filter(function (im) { const vids = im.variant_ids || []; if (!vids.length) return true; // gallery shot — applies to all colors return vids.some(function (id) { return idset[id]; }); }).map(function (im) { return im.src; }); return kept.length ? kept : all; } // v58 — token-overlap name match so "Butter Me Up Lace Bodysuit" matches the // vendor's "Butter Me Up Bodysuit". Fallback only, for when the style code // doesn't match (our internal code is rarely the vendor's number). function titleTokens(s) { return String(s || '').toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]/g, ' ').split(/\s+/).filter(function (w) { return w.length > 2; }); } function nameMatchScore(productTitle, wantName) { const a = titleTokens(productTitle), b = titleTokens(wantName); if (!a.length || !b.length) return 0; const aset = {}; a.forEach(function (w) { aset[w] = true; }); const hit = b.filter(function (w) { return aset[w]; }).length; return hit / b.length; } async function fetchVendorDetails(request, env) { const body = await request.json(); const vendor = body.vendor, styleCode = body.styleCode, handle = body.handle, productUrl = body.productUrl; const wantColor = body.color || ''; // v58 — colorway filter for images const wantName = body.name || ''; // v58 — name fallback for search const cfg = VENDOR_SITES[vendorKey(vendor)] || null; // v57 — resolution order: explicit body.domain > built-in VENDOR_SITES > // the in-app saved registry (D1 vendor_sites). Lets staff add any brand's // site once and have every future scanned product from it pull images. let saved = null; if (!body.domain && !cfg && vendor) { try { saved = await getVendorSite(env, 'miss-behavin', vendor); } catch (e) {} } const domain = String(body.domain || (cfg && cfg.domain) || (saved && saved.domain) || '').replace(/^https?:\/\//, '').replace(/\/.*$/, ''); const platform = (cfg && cfg.platform) || (saved && saved.platform) || body.platform || 'shopify'; // DETAIL mode — a specific Shopify product handle was chosen by staff. if (handle && domain && platform === 'shopify') { let res; try { res = await fetch('https://' + domain + '/products/' + encodeURIComponent(handle) + '.json', { headers: { 'User-Agent': VENDOR_FETCH_UA } }); } catch (e) { return json({ error: 'Fetch failed: ' + e.message }, 502); } if (!res.ok) return json({ error: 'Vendor product ' + res.status }, 502); const data = await res.json().catch(function () { return null; }); const p = data && data.product; if (!p) return json({ error: 'Product not found' }, 404); const desc = stripHtml(p.body_html); const ff = await extractFabricFit(env, desc); return json({ title: p.title, images: filterImagesByColor(p, wantColor), // v58 — only this colorway's shots images_all: (p.images || []).map(function (im) { return im.src; }), fabric: ff.fabric, fit: ff.fit, description: desc }); } // GENERIC fallback — a direct product URL for a non-Shopify site. if (productUrl) { // v32 — pasted URL: if it's a Shopify product URL (most of our vendors run // Shopify), read the public /products/.json FIRST — structured // title, vendor, price, color options, and ALL images, no scraping. Falls // through to the generic og:image / JSON-LD scrape for non-Shopify pages. try { const u = new URL(productUrl); const hm = u.pathname.match(/\/products\/([a-z0-9_-]+)/i); if (hm) { const jr = await fetch(u.origin + '/products/' + hm[1] + '.json', { headers: { 'User-Agent': VENDOR_FETCH_UA } }); if (jr.ok) { const jd = await jr.json().catch(function () { return null; }); const jp = jd && jd.product; if (jp) { const jdesc = stripHtml(jp.body_html); const jff = await extractFabricFit(env, jdesc); const copt = (jp.options || []).find(function (o) { return /colou?r/i.test(o.name || ''); }); return json({ title: jp.title, vendor: jp.vendor || '', price: (jp.variants && jp.variants[0] && jp.variants[0].price) || '', colors: (copt && copt.values) || [], images: (jp.images || []).map(function (im) { return im.src; }), fabric: jff.fabric, fit: jff.fit, description: jdesc }); } } } } catch (e) { /* fall through to the generic scrape */ } let res; try { res = await fetch(productUrl, { headers: { 'User-Agent': VENDOR_FETCH_UA } }); } catch (e) { return json({ error: 'Fetch failed: ' + e.message }, 502); } // v70 — blocked vendors (Free People and other bot-protected sites return // 403/401/429/451) get a plain-English message for the person at the form, // not a raw status code. blocked:true lets the app style it as a notice. if (!res.ok) { const BLOCKED = [401, 403, 429, 451]; if (BLOCKED.indexOf(res.status) !== -1) { return json({ error: "This vendor's website is blocking automatic import. Add this item manually below.", blocked: true }, 200); } return json({ error: 'Vendor page ' + res.status }, 502); } const html = await res.text(); const urls = new Set(); let m; const reOg = /]+property=["']og:image(?::secure_url)?["'][^>]+content=["']([^"']+)["']/gi; while ((m = reOg.exec(html))) urls.add(m[1]); const reLd = /"image"\s*:\s*(\[[^\]]*\]|"[^"]+")/gi; while ((m = reLd.exec(html))) { try { const v = JSON.parse(m[1]); (Array.isArray(v) ? v : [v]).forEach(function (u) { urls.add(u); }); } catch (e) {} } // v35 — pull the basics out of ANY product page, not just images: // og:title / for the name, og:site_name for the vendor, // og / product / JSON-LD price. All best-effort — whatever is found // prefills the manual row and the rest gets typed by hand. const meta1 = function (re) { const mm = html.match(re); return mm ? mm[1].trim() : ''; }; let gTitle = meta1(/<meta[^>]+property=["']og:title["'][^>]*content=["']([^"']+)["']/i) || meta1(/<title[^>]*>([^<]{2,140})<\/title>/i); let gVendor = meta1(/<meta[^>]+property=["']og:site_name["'][^>]*content=["']([^"']+)["']/i); const gPrice = meta1(/<meta[^>]+property=["'](?:og:price:amount|product:price:amount)["'][^>]*content=["']([0-9.]+)["']/i) || meta1(/"price"\s*:\s*"?([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]{1,2})?)"?/); // v70 — non-Shopify pages (Lucy In The Sky, boutiques) put the brand in the // page title as a " | BRAND" / " - BRAND" tail and leave og:site_name blank, // which shipped garbled names ("Front Twist Dress in Blue | LUCY IN THE SKY") // and no vendor/color. Clean all three server-side so the row prefills right. let gColor = meta1(/"color"\s*:\s*"([^"]{2,30})"/i); // JSON-LD / product color if (gTitle) { // 1) split off a trailing " | BRAND" or " - BRAND" tail; use it as the // vendor when og:site_name gave us nothing. const tailM = gTitle.match(/^(.*?)\s*[|–—\-]\s*([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9 .,'&]{1,38})\s*$/); if (tailM) { gTitle = tailM[1].trim(); if (!gVendor) gVendor = tailM[2].trim(); } // 2) "<Name> in <Color>" → pull the color out of the name. if (!gColor) { const inM = gTitle.match(/^(.*)\s+in\s+([A-Za-z][A-Za-z \-]{2,30})$/i); if (inM) { gTitle = inM[1].trim(); gColor = inM[2].trim(); } } else { // color known — strip a trailing " in <Color>" / " - <Color>" from name const cEsc = gColor.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&'); gTitle = gTitle.replace(new RegExp('\\s*(?:-|in)\\s*' + cEsc + '\\s*$', 'i'), '').trim() || gTitle; } } const desc = stripHtml(html).slice(0, 4000); const ff = await extractFabricFit(env, desc); return json({ title: gTitle, vendor: gVendor, price: gPrice, colors: gColor ? [gColor] : [], images: Array.from(urls).map(function (u) { return u.replace(/^\/\//, 'https://'); }).filter(function (u) { return /^https?:\/\//.test(u); }), fabric: ff.fabric, fit: ff.fit, description: desc }); } // SEARCH mode — find candidate products by style code. if (!domain) return json({ error: "Unknown vendor '" + (vendor || '') + "'. Add it to VENDOR_SITES, pass a domain, or use the Faire app for Faire brands." }, 400); if (platform !== 'shopify') return json({ error: 'Non-Shopify vendor: pass a productUrl for the generic fallback.' }, 400); if (!styleCode) return json({ error: 'Missing styleCode' }, 400); const matches = await shopifyFindByCode(domain, normCode(styleCode)); if (matches.length) return json({ candidates: matches.map(candidateFrom), matched_by: 'code' }); // v58 — code didn't match (our internal code is rarely the vendor's number). // Fall back to matching the invoice PRODUCT NAME against vendor titles. if (wantName) { const named = await shopifyFindByName(domain, wantName); if (named.length) return json({ candidates: named.map(candidateFrom), matched_by: 'name' }); } // v116 — the note reported `wantName || styleCode`, so whenever a name was // sent the STYLE-CODE search that ran first became invisible and the failure // read as "the app never tried the SKU". It always tries the SKU first. Say so. return json({ candidates: [], matched_by: 'none', note: 'No product matched style code "' + styleCode + '"' + (wantName ? ' or the invoice name "' + wantName + '"' : ' (the invoice printed no name to fall back on)') + ' on the vendor site (first ' + VENDOR_MAX_PAGES + ' pages). Paste the product URL directly.' }); } // v58 — scan the vendor catalog for products whose TITLE overlaps the invoice // product name. Returns the best few (score-sorted), never the whole catalog. async function shopifyFindByName(domain, wantName) { const scored = []; for (let page = 1; page <= VENDOR_MAX_PAGES; page++) { let res; try { res = await fetch('https://' + domain + '/products.json?limit=250&page=' + page, { headers: { 'User-Agent': VENDOR_FETCH_UA } }); } catch (e) { break; } if (!res.ok) break; const data = await res.json().catch(function () { return null; }); const products = (data && data.products) || []; if (!products.length) break; products.forEach(function (p) { const sc = nameMatchScore(p.title, wantName); if (sc >= 0.6) scored.push({ p: p, sc: sc }); }); if (products.length < 250) break; } scored.sort(function (a, b) { return b.sc - a.sc; }); return scored.slice(0, 6).map(function (x) { return x.p; }); } // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // THE EDIT — SEO/AEO blog drafts → HIDDEN Shopify articles (v3) // Drafts a post with Claude and saves it as a HIDDEN article under the blog // for human approval. Writes ARTICLES only. No auto-publish anywhere. // Reuses gql / anthropic / logChange / json / userErrs above. // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ const EDIT_CFG = { // Blog handle. Reusing the store's default "Miss Behavin' News" blog (handle // `news`). Override with env.EDIT_BLOG_HANDLE if you ever move blogs. DEFAULT_BLOG_HANDLE: 'news', // Blog byline / publisher name. A single named human (Sophie) for E-E-A-T. DEFAULT_BYLINE: "Sophie", MODEL: 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001', FROZEN_BRANDS: ["Rolla's", 'Gorjana'], MONEY_PAGES: [ { url: '/collections/free-people', label: 'Free People' }, { url: '/collections/motel-rocks', label: 'Motel Rocks' }, { url: '/collections/new-arrivals', label: 'New Arrivals' }, { url: '/collections/best-sellers', label: 'Best Sellers' }, { url: '/collections/dresses', label: 'Dresses' }, { url: '/collections/jeans', label: 'Jeans' }, { url: '/collections/swimwear', label: 'Swimwear' }, { url: '/collections/shoes', label: 'Shoes' }, { url: '/collections/jewelry-accessories', label: 'Jewelry & Accessories' }, { url: '/collections/sale', label: 'Sale' } // { url: '/pages/visit', label: 'Visit' } // [CONFIRM] add once the Visit page exists ] }; function editBlogHandle(env) { return (env && env.EDIT_BLOG_HANDLE) || EDIT_CFG.DEFAULT_BLOG_HANDLE; } // Public storefront origin for building live post links. Defaults to the // myshopify domain (which redirects to the primary domain). Set // env.EDIT_STOREFRONT_ORIGIN to the pretty domain (e.g. https://www.shopmissbehavin.com) for clean links. function editStorefrontOrigin(env) { if (env && env.EDIT_STOREFRONT_ORIGIN) return String(env.EDIT_STOREFRONT_ORIGIN).replace(/\/$/, ''); return 'https://' + String((env && env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN) || ''); } // Blog auth, 2026 model. Dev Dashboard apps no longer hand out permanent Admin // tokens; instead we exchange the app's Client ID + Secret for a 24h access // token (client credentials grant) and cache it in KV until shortly before it // expires. Docs: shopify.dev/docs/apps/build/dev-dashboard/get-api-access-tokens // Requires the "Miss Behavin Blog" app to be INSTALLED on the store, and the // app + store to be in the same Shopify organization (else shop_not_permitted). async function editContentToken(env) { const KEY = 'edit_cc_token'; try { const cached = env.CODES && await env.CODES.get(KEY, 'json'); if (cached && cached.t && Date.now() < cached.exp - 120e3) return cached.t; } catch (_) { /* cache miss is fine */ } const res = await fetch(`https://${env.SHOPIFY_DOMAIN}/admin/oauth/access_token`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' }, body: new URLSearchParams({ grant_type: 'client_credentials', client_id: env.SHOPIFY_BLOG_CLIENT_ID, client_secret: env.SHOPIFY_BLOG_CLIENT_SECRET }) }); if (!res.ok) { const t = await res.text(); throw new Error('Shopify token exchange ' + res.status + ': ' + t.slice(0, 300)); } const j = await res.json(); // { access_token, scope, expires_in: 86399 } const ttl = Math.max(60, (j.expires_in || 86399) - 300); try { if (env.CODES) await env.CODES.put(KEY, JSON.stringify({ t: j.access_token, exp: Date.now() + ttl * 1000 }), { expirationTtl: ttl }); } catch (_) { /* caching is best-effort */ } return j.access_token; } // Route the blog module's Shopify calls through DEDICATED content credentials, // so the main product token never needs write_content and is never touched by // this module. Preference order: client-credentials exchange (2026 model) -> // legacy SHOPIFY_CONTENT_TOKEN secret -> main SHOPIFY_TOKEN. async function editEnv(env) { // v41 — _TOKEN_PINNED tells gql() to use exactly this token and never // swap in the main client-credentials token (the blog module must keep // its dedicated content credentials). if (env && env.SHOPIFY_BLOG_CLIENT_ID && env.SHOPIFY_BLOG_CLIENT_SECRET) { const token = await editContentToken(env); return Object.assign({}, env, { SHOPIFY_TOKEN: token, _TOKEN_PINNED: true }); } if (!env || !env.SHOPIFY_CONTENT_TOKEN) return env; return Object.assign({}, env, { SHOPIFY_TOKEN: env.SHOPIFY_CONTENT_TOKEN, _TOKEN_PINNED: true }); } function editByline(env) { return (env && env.EDIT_BYLINE) || EDIT_CFG.DEFAULT_BYLINE; } // v94 — store_id for internal callers. editGenerate is reached two ways: an HTTP // request from the app, and a synthetic { json } object from editWeeklyAutoDraft // that has no .url at all. v85 added a bare `new URL(request.url)` here, which // throws TypeError on the synthetic one — so the weekly auto-draft failed on its // first line every time it got past the calendar gates since v85, logging an // error row nobody reads. Parse defensively, fall back to the default store. function editStoreId(request) { try { return (new URL(request.url)).searchParams.get('store_id') || MB_STORE_ID_DEFAULT; } catch (_) { return MB_STORE_ID_DEFAULT; } } function editModel(env) { return (env && env.EDIT_MODEL) || EDIT_CFG.MODEL; } // Frozen brands = the only hard blocklist. Any other real vendor is eligible, // so adding vendors in Shopify needs no code change. Override via env. function editFrozen(env) { const raw = (env && env.EDIT_FROZEN_BRANDS) ? String(env.EDIT_FROZEN_BRANDS).split(',') : EDIT_CFG.FROZEN_BRANDS; return raw.map(s => String(s).trim()).filter(Boolean); } function editNorm(s) { return String(s || '').toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]/g, ''); } function editIsFrozen(env, brand) { const n = editNorm(brand); return editFrozen(env).some(f => editNorm(f) === n); } async function editGenerate(request, env, session, isVariation) { env = await editEnv(env); const body = await request.json(); const brand = editPickBrand(env, body.brand, body.products); // throws if frozen/missing let siblings = []; try { siblings = await editRecentArticles(env); } catch (_) { siblings = []; } // v85 — the blog writer gets the researched phrases for its topic and its // featured brand, the same way the product writer gets them for a category. const _sid85 = editStoreId(request); const _topicTerms = await mbTermsForTopic(env, _sid85, [body.topic, body.angle, body.notes].filter(Boolean).join(' '), brand); const data = await anthropic(env, { model: editModel(env), max_tokens: 2500, temperature: isVariation ? 1.0 : 0.7, system: editSystemPrompt(brand, editFrozen(env), !!String((body && body.facts) || '').trim(), body.mode), messages: [{ role: 'user', content: editUserPrompt(body, brand, siblings, isVariation) + mbTopicPromptBlock(_topicTerms) }] }); const text = (data.content && data.content[0] && data.content[0].text) || ''; // Insert REAL product images (never invent URLs). Only arrivals mode carries them. let outText = text, _checks = null; try { const images = (body.products || []).map(p => ({ url: p.image, alt: p.title })).filter(i => i.url); const t = text.replace(/^```(?:json)?\s*/i, '').replace(/```\s*$/i, '').trim(); const draft = editParseLoose(t); // tolerant parse — repairs illegal escapes // Validate BEFORE the app inserts photographs, so the no-<img> check is // testing what the model wrote rather than what we added afterwards. _checks = editValidate(draft, { frozen: editFrozen(env), byline: String((body && body.byline) || '').trim() || editByline(env), allowedUrls: EDIT_CFG.MONEY_PAGES.map(p => p.url).concat(siblings.map(s => s.url)) }); if (images.length) draft.body_html = editInsertImages(draft.body_html, images); outText = JSON.stringify(draft); // the client now ALWAYS receives valid JSON } catch (_) { return json({ error: 'The draft came back malformed. Tap Generate Draft again \u2014 a fresh attempt usually lands.' }, 502); } try { await logChange(env, { user: (session && session.name) || 'unknown', action: 'edit_generate', field: 'edit', item_title: editTitleFrom(text) || (isVariation ? 'variation' : (body.topic || brand)), old_value: '', new_value: (isVariation ? 'variation drafted' : 'draft generated') }); } catch (_) {} // v86 — `registry` travels back so the draft screen can name the researched // phrases that fed the post, or say plainly that none matched the topic. // `text` keeps its exact shape, so every existing caller is unaffected. // v96 — `had_facts` lets the draft screen say plainly whether this post was // written from real material or from shape alone. Same reasoning as the // researched-words chips: never leave the reviewer guessing. return json({ text: outText, registry: _topicTerms, checks: _checks, had_facts: !!String((body && body.facts) || '').trim() }); // client strips fences + JSON.parse (existing convention) } // v97 — THE VALIDATOR. Nine of the sixteen self-checks in the prompt are // mechanically decidable, and a small model asked to police sixteen rules and // silently repair failures will miss some. Code does not miss. These run after // generation and travel back to the draft screen, so the reviewer sees exactly // what is wrong instead of being asked to trust that the model checked itself. // Nothing here blocks or edits the draft — a human still decides. It only tells // the truth about what came back. const EDIT_BANNED = ['literally', 'trousers', 'delve', 'elevate your', 'must-have', 'game-changer', 'look no further', "in today's world", 'effortlessly', 'timeless piece', 'obsessed']; function editValidate(draft, ctx) { const fail = [], warn = []; const html = String((draft && draft.body_html) || ''); const text = html.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, ' '); const low = (text + ' ' + (draft.title || '') + ' ' + (draft.meta_title || '') + ' ' + (draft.meta_description || '')).toLowerCase(); const cp = s => [...String(s || '').normalize('NFC')].length; // 1 — banned vocabulary EDIT_BANNED.forEach(w => { if (low.indexOf(w) >= 0) fail.push('Banned wording present: "' + w + '"'); }); // 2 — frozen brands, the rule with a business consequence (ctx.frozen || []).forEach(b => { if (editNorm(low).indexOf(editNorm(b)) >= 0) fail.push('FROZEN BRAND named: ' + b); }); // 3 — no images; the app inserts real photos afterwards if (/<img\b/i.test(html)) fail.push('Contains an <img> tag. Photos are inserted by the app, never written here'); if (/<(script|style|html|head|body)\b/i.test(html)) fail.push('Contains a disallowed tag (script/style/html/head/body)'); // 4 — links: counts, both-places, and no invented URLs const arr = Array.isArray(draft.links) ? draft.links : []; const hrefs = []; const re = /<a\s[^>]*href\s*=\s*["']([^"']+)["']/gi; let m; while ((m = re.exec(html))) hrefs.push(m[1]); const money = arr.filter(l => l && l.type === 'money'); const sib = arr.filter(l => l && l.type === 'sibling'); const typed = arr.filter(l => l && (l.type === 'money' || l.type === 'sibling')); if (typed.length !== arr.length) fail.push('A links entry is missing its type'); if (money.length !== 1) fail.push('Expected exactly 1 money-page link, found ' + money.length); if (sib.length > 2) fail.push('Expected 0 to 2 sibling links, found ' + sib.length); const allowed = (ctx.allowedUrls || []).map(u => String(u).replace(/\/$/, '')); arr.forEach(l => { const u = String((l && l.url) || '').replace(/\/$/, ''); if (!u) { fail.push('A links entry has no url'); return; } if (allowed.length && allowed.indexOf(u) < 0) fail.push('Invented URL not on the supplied lists: ' + u); if (hrefs.map(h => h.replace(/\/$/, '')).indexOf(u) < 0) fail.push('Link is in the array but not in the body: ' + u); }); hrefs.forEach(h => { const u = h.replace(/\/$/, ''); if (arr.map(l => String((l && l.url) || '').replace(/\/$/, '')).indexOf(u) < 0) fail.push('Link is in the body but not in the array: ' + h); }); // 5 — byline is never the model's to choose if (ctx.byline && String(draft.author || '').trim() !== String(ctx.byline).trim()) { fail.push('Author is "' + (draft.author || '') + '", expected "' + ctx.byline + '"'); } if (ctx.byline && text.indexOf(ctx.byline) < 0) warn.push('The byline does not appear in the body'); // 6 — metadata, counted the way the validator counts (NFC code points) if (cp(draft.meta_title) > 70) fail.push('meta_title is ' + cp(draft.meta_title) + ' characters, limit 70'); if (cp(draft.meta_description) > 160) fail.push('meta_description is ' + cp(draft.meta_description) + ' characters, limit 160'); if (/"/.test(String(draft.meta_title || '') + String(draft.meta_description || ''))) fail.push('A meta field contains a double quote'); // 7 — structure. Warnings, because judging a "comparison" is not a code job. const h2 = html.match(/<h2\b[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/h2>/gi) || []; if (!h2.length) fail.push('No H2 headings at all'); else if (!/\?\s*<\/h2>/i.test(h2[0])) warn.push('The opening H2 is not phrased as a question'); if (h2.length < 3) warn.push('Only ' + h2.length + ' H2 section(s) — check it is not thin'); // 8 — hoco is required on a homecoming post, per the vocabulary rules if (/homecoming|hoco/.test(low) && String(draft.title || '').toLowerCase().indexOf('hoco') < 0) { fail.push('Homecoming post, but "hoco" is missing from the title'); } // 9 — first person about experience the writer does not have if (/\b(i|we) (wore|tried|felt|owned|styled it|put it on)\b/i.test(text)) { fail.push('First-person claim of having worn or tried something'); } // 10 - v123: AN UNANSWERED BRACKET IS A QUESTION FOR A HUMAN AND MAY NOT // LEAVE HERE AS COPY. On 2026-08-28 "Do stripes really make you look // slimmer?" published carrying "[PLACEHOLDER. Three or four real // observations from the shop go here...]" under its own heading on the live // blog. TWO separate faults let that through and both are fixed. First, the // placeholder rule that should have caught it lives in the PRODUCT/metadata // rule set, not in this one - the blog path had no such check at all. // Second, that rule's pattern is /\[CONFIRM:|\[FILL\]|\bTODO\b|lorem ipsum/, // which does not contain the word PLACEHOLDER, so it would have passed the // exact string that shipped even if it had been applied here. // The marker set below is deliberately identical to the one app build 169 // blocks Publish on, so the Worker and the app can never disagree about what // counts as unfinished. Short brackets - [1], [sic] - are left alone. const slotRe = /\[([^\[\]]{2,800})\]/g; const slotWords = /(PLACEHOLDER|\bTO ?DO\b|\bCONFIRM\b|\bBLANK\b|\bFILL\b|\bINSERT\b|YOUR WORDS|LOREM IPSUM|\bTK\b|\bXXX+\b|\bTBD\b)/i; const slotsSeen = []; let slotM; while ((slotM = slotRe.exec(html))) { const inner = String(slotM[1]).trim(); if (!slotWords.test(inner) && inner.length < 12) continue; slotsSeen.push(inner.length > 60 ? inner.slice(0, 60) + '\u2026' : inner); } if (slotsSeen.length) { fail.push(slotsSeen.length + ' unanswered placeholder' + (slotsSeen.length === 1 ? '' : 's') + ' left in the body: "' + slotsSeen.join('", "') + '". Write the section from supplied facts, or omit the section and its heading. Never ship a bracket.'); } return { fail, warn, ok: fail.length === 0 }; } // Claude occasionally emits JSON containing illegal escapes (e.g. \' inside // "'70s"). Parse strictly first; on failure, strip every backslash that does // not begin a legal JSON escape sequence and parse once more. function editParseLoose(t) { try { return JSON.parse(t); } catch (_) { const repaired = String(t).replace(/\\(?!["\\\/bfnrtu]|u[0-9a-fA-F]{4})/g, ''); return JSON.parse(repaired); } } // Put a hero image at the top of the post and any extras in a gallery near the // end. Uses only the real URLs passed in; the model never sees or invents them. function editInsertImages(html, images) { if (!images || !images.length) return html; const q = s => String(s || '').replace(/"/g, '"'); const hero = images[0]; let out = '<p><img src="' + hero.url + '" alt="' + q(hero.alt) + '" style="width:100%;height:auto;"/></p>\n' + (html || ''); if (images.length > 1) { const rest = images.slice(1).map(im => '<img src="' + im.url + '" alt="' + q(im.alt) + '" style="width:100%;height:auto;margin-bottom:10px;"/>' ).join('\n'); out += '\n<div>\n' + rest + '\n</div>'; } return out; } // Product photos for the in-app image picker. Newest active products first. // // v113 — SEARCHABLE AND PAGED. Until now this took one argument, brand, and // returned a hard 24 photos with no way to ask for anything else. An evergreen // post has no brand, so it fell to "newest 20 active products", which on // 2026-08-16 meant bracelets and cropped pants on a homecoming-dress post and // no control anywhere to go and find a dress. Three things changed: // // category — Shopify product_type, the same field Manual Entry calls Category // q — free text across title, vendor, tag and SKU (Shopify's own search) // cursor — real pagination, so Load more keeps going instead of stopping at 24 // // Every filter is optional and they combine. Frozen brands are still dropped // whatever the filters say: an explicit frozen brand is refused up front, and a // frozen vendor reached through a category or text search is skipped in the loop. async function editImages(request, env) { env = await editEnv(env); const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({})); const brandRaw = String((body && body.brand) || '').trim(); const catRaw = String((body && body.category) || '').trim(); const textRaw = String((body && body.q) || '').trim(); const cursor = (body && body.cursor) ? String(body.cursor) : null; if (brandRaw && editIsFrozen(env, brandRaw)) return json({ error: 'This brand is frozen and cannot be featured.' }, 400); const PER_PAGE = 24; // products fetched per page const PER_PROD = 3; // photos taken from each product // Shopify product search syntax.
